<!-- page 38 -->

37
TOGGLE’S TEMPLE
T
oggle’s Temple
t o g g l e s t e m p l e t o g g l e s
The Temple is an underground armory, gun range, and 
configurable weapons-based obstacle course located 
in a tangled collection of old sewers, abandoned 
basements, and broken underground parking garages 
beneath the western edge of the Hot Zone. The customer 
entrance is hidden in one of Little Europe’s sewer tunnels, 
three blocks north of Short Circuit (see CP:R page 312). 
Gaining access to The Temple requires an introduction 
from an existing customer or a personal invitation from 
Toggle. Either can be gained via roleplay or, at the GM’s 
discretion, via passing a DV21 Streetwise Check. Feel free 
to increase the DV if the person seeking access works for 
the police or a Megacorp (two of Toggle’s “three Cs”) or 
to reduce the DV to account for a Fixer’s Contacts.
Toggle charges 20eb (Everyday) per head per visit. 
Once admitted, visitors can borrow any weapon from the 
armory and use it in either the firing range or in the more 
elaborate obstacle course, which is modeled after the 
“crime houses” commonly used to test SWAT teams. Every 
gun comes fully loaded with Basic Ammunition (or what-
ever ammunition required for use) at no charge. Additional 
ammunition can be purchased from vendits found through-
out the facility. Visitors must return all borrowed firearms 
before leaving The Temple, and they are responsible for 
reimbursing costs if a weapon is broken or destroyed.
Toggle runs The Temple by himself. The facility is well 
protected by various defenses, but he has no human 
muscle to enforce the rules. Security for The Temple is 
provided primarily by the secrecy of the location and 
the loyalty of its visitors. Many members of Night City’s 
edgerunning community appreciate the services pro-
vided by The Temple and will think poorly of anyone 
who messes with it.
T
oggle’s Armory
t o g g l
e
s a r m o r y a r m o r y
The following is an incomplete list of firearms avail-
able for use in The Temple. Toggle is a collector whose 
armory can hold any weapon the GM desires.
Cost: The monetary cost of the weapons presented in 
Toggle’s Temple might be more than their assigned Cost 
Category. Use the monetary cost when determining how 
many eb to spend and the Cost Category to determine 
the difficulty of sourcing (aka finding) the weapon.
Slots: All the weapons in Toggle’s Temple are 
non-Exotic, so we’ve added a Slots category to each 
listing. This isn’t something new; It acts as a visual 
representation of how many slots are available for 
Attachments upon purchase. Some of these weapons 
come with Attachments already installed. Pre-installed 
Attachments can be removed, freeing the slot they take 
up, but can only be transferred to other weapons of 
the same type. In other words, no, your Heavy Pistol’s 
drum magazine won’t magically grow in size to fit an 
Assault Rifle.

doorstop. The Convenience comes in a variety of ammo 
“flavors” with cost depending on what’s loaded inside. 
Collect them all!
A Standard Quality Very Heavy Pistol found in 
vendits across Night City. To prevent accidents while 
in storage, the weapon must first be activated by 
removing a pull-tab, which does not require an Action. 
The Convenience comes preloaded with a specific 
ammunition type depending on its “flavor” and cannot 
be reloaded once empty. Attempting to extract the 
ammunition ruins the weapon and the ammo inside, 
Destroying both Beyond Repair. The Convenience 
cannot be Tech Upgraded but will accept all compat-
ible non-magazine Attachments, so remove anything 
you installed before throwing the gun away!
 
X Federated Arms Super Chief Plus
Cost: 50eb (Costly)
Slots: 3
When Nova Arms released a full line of “Cityhunter” 
firearms, their rival Federated Arms responded by 
expanding the “Super Chief” brand. Unfortunately, 
they skimped on quality as they rushed the design 
process to get to market, creating an inferior semi-au-
tomatic pistol.
A Poor Quality Very Heavy Pistol.
 
X Militech Boomer Buster
Cost: 500eb (Expensive)
Slots: 3
Customer feedback told Militech their .477 Boomer 
Buster was a hit with Solos looking for a high-quality 
sidearm. The only complaint was how often the gun 
needed to be reloaded. With that in mind, Militech 
engineers reworked the gun to increase the ammo 
capacity, resulting in the Boomer Buster we all know 
and love today.
An Excellent Quality Very Heavy Pistol.
 
X Nomad Big Gulp
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Slots: 3
Double-barreled. Fits in your truck’s cup holder and sits 
low in your holster. What more can you ask for? Not 
much more than you’re gonna get. Because they are 
manufactured by a plethora of weaponsmiths across 
the various nomad nations, each Big Gulp has a unique 
look to them.
An Excellent Quality Very Heavy Pistol with a 2-shot 
capacity. It can load two different types of ammunition, 
one in each barrel. When firing the Big Gulp, the user 
chooses which ammunition to use. Incompatible with 
all magazine Attachments.

X Mustang Arms Rodeo

X Chadran Arms City Reaper
Cost: 50eb (Costly)
Slots: 3

X IMI UZ 2045 Pro
Cost: 600eb (Expensive)
Slots: 1

an installed Airhypo Bayonet.

An Excellent Quality Assault Rifle. It comes equipped

An Excellent Quality Sniper Rifle.

Cost: 100eb (Premium)

X Airhypo Bayonet
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All Non-Exotic Ranged Weapons fired with 
the Shoulder Arms Skill.
A drug-based twist on the standard bayonet. You won’t 
break flesh with this attachment, but you can pump someone 
full of whatever chemical you load into the reservoir.
An easy-to-use drug distribution platform mounted onto 
the end of a firearm. Instead of doing damage on a hit, 
the Airhypo Bayonet uses a quick burst of compressed 
air to force a liquid through the skin, delivering a dose 
of the loaded substance to the target. If the Melee 
Weapon Attack Check fails, the dose is not lost. The 
Airhypo’s reservoir can hold up to one dose of a single 
substance. Reloading the reservoir requires an Action.
 
X Grapple Gun Underbarrel
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: Non-Exotic Crossbows and all Non-Exotic 
Ranged Weapons fired with the Shoulder Arms Skill.
When someone shoves you off a building, you don’t 
always have time to drop your rifle and grab your 
grapple gun. Save your life by combining the two!
The weapon can be used as a Grapple Gun (see 
CP:R, page 353). However, it cannot make Attack 
Checks while being used as a Grapple Gun.
 
X Silencer
Cost: 100eb
Eligible: All Non-Exotic Non-Explosive Ranged 
Weapons, excluding Bows & Crossbows.
Far more than just a suppressor, a silencer uses a 
combination of sound baffling and noise-cancellation 
technology to make a firearm whisper-quiet without 
sacrificing power. Perfect for when you don’t want your 
target to know they’re being attacked until it’s too late.
Hearing a shot from a weapon equipped with a 
Silencer requires a Perception Check against a DV 
determined by the GM. When determining the DV, the 
GM should consider the weapon’s size, the distance 
between the listener and the shooter, and any ambient 
noise. Silencers do not stop anyone from noticing 
damage from a bullet’s impact, only the shot itself.

???: Uh, Mister 7? A friend of a friend said you were 
looking for some info about the docks. I, er, I might 
have something for you, maybe?
24/7: You heard correctly, and please, 24/7, or 
Toofer, if it makes you more comfortable. And who am 
I talking to?
Barry (B): … <static> ...Barry. I’m Barry.
24/7: Ok, Barry, what would you like to share with 
me?
B: My… friend… said you were looking into some things 
going missing? Cargo ain’t where it’s supposed to be, 
that kind of thing.
24/7: And you have something for me on that front. 
I’m listening. Unless you’d prefer to meet?
B: No! <static> No, this is better. Safer. I don’t want to 
draw any attention. See, we’ve had an old freighter 
come in, small, maybe a dozen containers. Salvage, 
y’know?
24/7: I understand.
B: Well, we’d normally get to offload and uncrate. 
No good to the owner if it just sits there on the docks. 
But we got told to leave the stuff as-is, stacked by the 
water.
24/7: And that’s unusual?
B: Oh, yeah, for sure. Usually can’t clear it fast enough, 
especially if it’s construction gear. And that’s what the 
labels scanned out as. Builders want their stuff, you 
know?
24/7: Barry, I sense there’s something more than 
unusual cargo handling here.
B: Well, next morning, got our job sheet and suddenly 
the “do not touch” is off two of the containers. No 
pickup, no papers, just clear them off the stack. Ain’t 
none of my business, I did. And they were empty.
24/7: You’re sure?
B: Sure I’m sure, I know the difference between han-
dling a loaded and empty container. I mentioned it to 
my buddy Karel after work, and he said he’d had to 
clean them out. Whole inside was coated in a film of… 
something. He didn’t know what, but needed crazy 
solvents to clear.
24/7: Karel…big choomba, blonde spikes in his hair?
B: Yeah. He said you were asking questions about what 
was in those containers. I know he didn’t say nothing, 
because how’d he know anything? But he was pulling a 
double that night, said he’d keep his eyes open, maybe 
get something for you if you were still paying.
24/7: Yes, Barry, I’m very much in the market for…
B: Mister Toofer, Karel didn’t show up this morning. 
He clocked in, but not out, last night. And this morning 
another pair of containers need cleaning from that 
stack… <static>
24/7: Barry, are you all right?
B: I think I might be in trouble, Mister Toofer. I took a 
sample of the crap I was cleaning out of those contain-
ers of “construction equipment,” and it’s some weird oil 
that colored my radiation badge.
24/7: Barry, I’ll pay a finder’s fee for that sample…
B: Yeah, great, it’s in my place… sending you the 
address… in a Smash can under the bed. I might not 
be there, though. I think someone’s been following me 
since I worked on that job. My Agent ain’t great, but I 
got a picture. Sending it now. See it?
24/7: I do, Barry, this is great, thank you. I’ll try to 
identify…
B: Yeah, great and all, but …<static>... I think he’s back. 
Mr. Toofer, I’m scared. Can you help me?
24/7: Barry, yes, we need to meet. I can help you…
B: …<static>...
24/7: Barry? Are you there? Can you hear me?
B: … <static>...

he Investigation System
t h e i n v e s t i g a t i o n s y s t e m
Combat in Cyberpunk RED can break out at the drop 
of a grenade pin, but Friday Night Firefights aren’t the only 
way to build tension and create drama in Night City.

<!-- page 64 -->

63
DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
Adding investigations to the mix can deepen the game-
play experience, give the less up-front Roles a chance to 
shine, and really dig into the noir roots of the genre.
Writing and planning mysteries can be tricky, especially if 
you’ve never crafted a whodunit before. Often, what seems 
obvious and straightforward in a GM’s  head feels obtuse 
and obfuscated to the Players. The entirely optional 
Investigation System is here to help, giving GMs a leg up on 
devising, plotting, and resolving mysteries and investigative 
elements. The mechanics presented here aren’t intended to 
replace roleplay but will lead a group through the process 
of investigation without getting bogged down because 
they don’t have the deductive prowess of one of Dashiell 
Hammett’s black-and-white detectives.
Using the Investigation System below, you’ll design 
your mystery and assign Clues for the Edgerunners to 
discover. In return, your Players will use this evidence to 
follow the trail and come to a conclusion about “who 
done it,” complete with failsafes in case the Crew turns 
out to be better at coming up with answers than their 
Players. We begin with Focus, a new Derived Statistic.
Focus
Just like your body, your mind has its limits and inves-
tigating mysteries is going to test them. Focus is meant 
to represent those limits. You can only push your mind 
so far before you run out of bandwidth and just can’t 
concentrate or conclude any further without rest.
The Formula for determining your Focus is 
10 + 5[(INT + WILL)/2 (round up)]
Hitting 0 Focus represents an inability to properly 
make sense of clues or concentrate on mysteries as a 
whole. Until the Focus rises back above 0, they will 
be unable to make Evidence Checks (see page 64). 
Edgerunners only have one Focus pool, even if they are 
investigating multiple mysteries. In other words, if you’re 
following a few different sleuthing plots at once, each 
deals damage to the same Focus.
Focus recovers upon resting for a day and doing 
nothing else, such as Hustling or using Role Abilities. 
Whenever you recover Hit Points from resting, you also 
recover an amount of Focus equal to your INT + WILL. 
With a successful DV15 Concentration Check, you 
recover an additional 5 Focus while resting.
Mysteries
Mysteries are as varied as the people involved but do 
require common components to function. Specifically, 
each Mystery has a Goal and Complexity.
Goal: This is the end state of the Mystery, such as figur-
ing out who stole an experimental piece of cyberware, 
identifying the assailant in a hit-and-run, or piecing 
together the chemical composition of a new street drug.
Complexity: The Complexity of a Mystery indicates 
how hard it is to solve, serving as Hit Points of sorts. 
Each clue deciphered via an Evidence Check deals 
damage to a Mystery’s Complexity. Once a Mystery’s 
Complexity reaches 0, the Crew has everything they 
need to solve it.

DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
 
X Mystery Complexity
Difficulty
Complexity
Example
Easy
25
The Mystery is only one part of a single Mission, such as deciphering 
the location of an upcoming Night Market.
Average
50
The Mystery forms the basis of or a large part of a single Mission, such 
as working out who killed the Crew’s Fixer.
Challenging
100
The Mystery is ongoing and meant to be solved over multiple Missions, 
such as working out who hired the assassin who killed the Crew’s Fixer.
Difficult
150
The Mystery is ongoing and an important subplot for the campaign or 
an individual Edgerunner, such as discovering the whereabouts of a 
Lover listed as missing during a Crew member’s Lifepath.
Legendary
200
The Mystery is ongoing and underpins the whole campaign, such as 
discovering the truth behind who knocked the Crew out and trans-
ported them from New York to Night City … and why they did it.
Not every Mystery can be solved as soon as it’s 
given. Some Mysteries are long-term and worked on if 
and when an Edgerunner receives a new lead or carves 
out some free time. For longer-term mysteries, consider 
planning for when new clues will be made available, 
especially after significant events or Missions. Clues 
could easily be a part of a gig's payment, helping 
reignite interest after a dry spell without any leads. 
Think about a television drama with a season-long 
plot. The mystery continues in the background, even 
if an episode doesn’t directly relate to the overarching 
plotline.
Clues and Evidence Checks
Clues represent the pieces to the puzzle that make 
up every Mystery. To collect a clue and discern its 
relevance, Players make an Evidence Check using 
an appropriate Skill against a DV determined by the 
GM.  If they succeed, they collect the Clue and make 
a damage roll, subtracting the result from the Mystery’s 
Complexity. If the Crew fails to collect the Clue, they 
take damage to their Focus. If the Edgerunner rolls a 
“1” and also fails the Check (called a Fumble for this 
purpose), additional complications can occur. A Solo’s 
Fumble Recovery does not negate the negative effects 
of rolling a 1 in this case.
The Clues on the Clue Table (see page 65) are 
examples. GMs should feel free to make up their own 
using these as a guideline.
Each clue has an Obfuscation rating. Consider this 
the clue’s Armor SP, reducing the damage dealt by 
solving an Evidence Check. Obfuscation represents 
the difficulty of obtaining useful information from the 
clue, either due to situational barriers or intentional 
interference by an outside party.

<!-- page 67 -->

66
DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
Solving a Mystery
To solve a Mystery, the Crew needs to 
make Evidence Checks, deal damage, and 
bring the Mystery’s Complexity down to 0. 
Once they do, they’ve cracked the case.
Each Edgerunner involved in an investi-
gation can make one Evidence Check per 
Clue per day. If an Edgerunner fails, they 
can try again on another day, but the DV or 
Obfuscation rating might change to represent 
the passage of time. As long as one Crew 
member succeeds at an Evidence Check, the 
Clue is collected and the damage is dealt to 
the Mystery’s Complexity, although anyone 
who failed still loses Focus.
Evidence Checks don’t have a set duration 
and can take as long as the GM decides 
they need to take.  Some can be as quick 
as a single Action, such as glancing over a 
crime scene to notice something is amiss. 
Others could require a full day of research 
to discern the actual value of the Clue. 
Edgerunners can’t just go around guessing 
that something’s a clue, though. They need a 
reason to perform an Evidence Check. 
To perform an Evidence Check, an 
Edgerunner must make an appropriate 
Skill Check. Don’t feel limited by the Skills 
listed in the Clue table. As always, follow 
the Rule of Cool. If a Player can justify an 
alternative Skill, go for it. If the Check is 
successful, they deal the listed damage 
to the Mystery’s Complexity (minus the 
Obfuscation rating). Edgerunners who fail 
an Evidence Check take damage to their 
Focus as noted in the Clue’s entry on the 
Clue table.
If the Players are feeling aimless and 
unsure of where to go next, allow them 
to make a DV15 Deduction Check. If they 
succeed, give them a hint as to where 
another Clue might be found. Whenever 
an Edgerunner makes this type of Check, 
they take 1d6 Focus damage, representing 
the mental strain of racking their brains for 
any possible solution.
Sometimes, the Players will solve a mystery 
before their Characters do. When that 
happens, it is up to the GM to decide how 
they wish to proceed. It could be that, despite 
figuring out “who done it,” the local author-
ities or their employer need more evidence 
before they can act. Or it could be the GM 
agrees, slashes the mystery’s Complexity 
down to 0, and declares it solved. The GM 
could even change the question underpinning 
the mystery. For example, from “Who killed 
the disc jockey?” to “Where is the disc jock-
ey’s killer hiding?” It is up to the GM to decide 
on the best course of action for their table.
The Players can also stuff it up and point 
the finger at the wrong suspect. The GM 
doesn’t need to tell them they’re off track 
and still have more Complexity to mark off. 
False accusations are all part of the genre, 
after all! If the Players realize they’re on the 
wrong path, they can veer back. And if they 
don’t? That’s more plot fodder for the GM 
down the line. A choomba falsely accused 
of murder can make for a dangerous enemy!
Obstacles
No Mystery is complete without compli-
cations, represented in the Investigation 
System by Obstacles. 
Obstacles exist to add depth to the 
mystery. They will also play a part in 
taking a bite out of an Edgerunner’s Focus, 
representing the stress and frustration that 
comes when unwanted interruptions stymie 
a perfectly good Mystery. Obstacles are 
optional and should only be used when it 
feels thematically appropriate and logical.
To deploy an Obstacle, determine its 
nature, and then decide if overcoming it 
requires a simple Skill Check (against a DV 
of the GM’s choosing) or a new Beat involv-
ing multiple Skill Checks and interactions. 
Edgerunners who overcome an Obstacle 
take 1d6 Focus damage. If they fail to over-
come the Obstacle, they take 2d6 Focus 
damage instead. 
Long Term 
Mysteries
When solving a mystery, 
edgerunners don’t always 
need to commit to the 
task doggedly, making 
the maximum number of 
Evidence Checks per day. 
While some mysteries may 
demand such dedication, 
others are situational and 
specifically long-term. An 
Edgerunner looking for 
someone who killed her 
lover five years ago may 
make a single Evidence 
Check per week as a side 
project when not focusing 
on other jobs. If what she 
discovers opens up new, 
immediate avenues of 
investigation she can keep 
going. Otherwise, she’ll 
wait to follow up later. Long 
term mysteries branching 
off in-game events or an 
Edgerunner’s Lifepath 
make for terrific ongoing 
background plots.


---

<!-- page 68 -->

67
DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
 
X Obstacle Examples
Obstacle Type
Example
Authority
Corporations or other powerful individuals impede progress.
Cultural Disputes
Differences in cultural understanding or customs obscure information.
Digital
Information is locked away in hard-to-access net architectures.
Distraction
Another event makes it difficult to focus on the investigation.
Fatigue
Physical and mental exhaustion makes progress difficult.
Legal
Police or other peacekeepers deny access to vital parts of the case.
Location
The case takes place in a remote or otherwise hard-to-reach place.
Misdirection
Information on the case is muddied in waves of digital gossip.
Missing Clue
A Clue simply isn’t available at the moment and that lack is frustrating, wearing 
down Focus. For example, someone who needs to be questioned won’t be 
accessible until tomorrow, or the characters are looking in entirely the wrong 
place for evidence.
Red Herring
Something that looks like a Clue isn’t. Red herrings should never be presented to 
the Players as an Obstacle. Instead, present them as an Evidence check. If they 
beat the DV, they realize the red herring isn’t a Clue (for this mystery, at least). If 
they fail, tell them they haven’t deciphered the Clue and roll damage as if they 
had failed an Evidence check.
Social Engineering
Someone is manipulating the flow of data about the incident.
Technological Issues
Technology in the case is proving difficult to interface with due to poor conditions 
or unfamiliarity.
Territory Disputes
The area of the case is unable to be accessed due to gangs or other violence.
▶ The Ticking Clock ◀
Sometimes a mystery will be time-sensitive, and that 
is its own form of Obstacle: the Ticking Clock. In this 
situation, the Edgerunners are up against a deadline 
they may or may not be aware of. “Deliver the ransom 
before X time or the hostage will be returned in a 
selection of fast food containers” has a clearly defined 
time pressure in which to track down and rescue said 
hostage (or raise the ransom). However, tracking down 
a Netrunner who has stolen a client’s data before they 
can arrange safe passage out of Night City isn’t as 
clear-cut. Either way, a Ticking Clock is an excellent 
way to sharpen and drive forward an investigation, 
especially if the Players are aware of their time limit.
Crafting a Mystery
When you design a mystery, you need a starting point 
and a conclusion. Once you nail those down, you can 
build the chain of clues and deductions that link the 
two. It’s down to personal taste as to whether you start 
from the hook and work through to the end or start 
at the conclusion and work backward, ostensibly the 
preferred method of one Agatha Christie, no slouch in 
the mystery-making department.
Your clues will normally fall into one of the categories 
detailed above, but feel free to create your own cate-
gories using similar difficulty scales as needed. When 
you’ve completed your chain of clues, you’ll have a 
clear path leading from incident to denouement – 
assuming the Players look in the right places, ask the 
right questions, and talk to the right people. It’s your 
“ideal” version of how the investigation will progress.
Once you introduce the mystery to people you’re 
not in control of (that is, the Players), your ideal inves-
tigation may get significantly fuzzier, whether due to 
a series of bad rolls or because they miss something 
you believed was blindingly obvious. Conversely, the 
Players may achieve an intuitive leap or randomly


---

<!-- page 69 -->

68
DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
guess a solution and skip a whole chunk of 
your carefully constructed chain ahead of 
schedule. Either scenario can be frustrating, 
for both the GM and the Players.
To help your Mystery run smoothly, 
ensure that the first clue in your chain is 
easy to find and interpret. If your Players 
are more used to combat-heavy games, 
mysteries may be something new and you 
don’t want them to spend the first half hour 
staring up at the metaphorical climbing 
wall looking for the a foothold. In the case 
of a murder, give them a scene of crime 
or a body to look into; for any other cases 
(for example, theft or kidnapping), there 
should be a point of origin for the crime, 
somewhere they can be pointed to either 
because it’s dropped on them or because 
they were hired to look into it. Consider 
noir detective fiction and how often a 
client (who is often distractingly attractive 
and possibly a point of Obfuscation or Red 
Herring in their own right) saunters up to the 
protagonist and drops a case on their desk. 
Don’t be afraid to use the classics. Tropes 
become tropes for a reason.
Once they have one source of clues 
under their belts, the Edgerunners will often 
find the next naturally, forming a cascading 
chain. They investigate a crime scene where 
someone was murdered; this might lead 
them to a witness who runs the food cart 
nearby or a friendly cop who gives them 
access to the body for further study. Maybe 
their search of the crime scene turned up the 
murder weapon, and they can then look into 
that, including trace evidence or who might 
have had access. In this way, your chain 
can become a web, with each clue offering 
branching links to others, all of which can be 
used to dent the mystery’s Complexity rating 
and bring them closer to a solution.
Something to avoid is the single point of 
failure. When you put together your ideal 
clue chain, look at each element, and 
ask yourself “How many different ways 
can they deal with this?” If the answer is, 
for example, one specific Skill Check at 
exactly the right time and place, you may 
need to build in some redundancy because 
you’ve hidden the rest of the story behind 
a Skill paywall. That single point of failure 
could lock the Players out of the rest of 
the mystery, forcing you to use a deus ex 
machina to get them back on track. Not 
very satisfying for you or them.
To avoid this brick wall, try and stay 
flexible. This doesn’t mean you have to 
account for every possible alternative way 
for the Edgerunners to gain the essential 
information they need to progress, but 
you should consider your options. Maybe 
the neatest clue is to be found by sweet-
talking the right bartender who overheard 
the villains planning their crime and can 
describe them to a T, but what happens 
if the Edgerunners didn’t visit that bar, or 
were abrasive to the bartender, or the 
poor drink-slinger caught a stray bullet 
completely by accident?
In such a case, consider who else might 
have witnessed the conversation. Maybe 
a server overheard part of the plot but 
doesn’t remember the baddies’ faces. 
A streetrat might have tried to hustle 
them outside, caught a beating for their 
trouble, and be keen to leap in with what 
they know. A local influencer wannabe 
could have been livecasting at the bar 
and recorded more than they bargained 
for. You don’t have to nail every option 
down, but keep an open mind, espe-
cially if the Edgerunners start employing 
lateral thinking. There are almost always 
multiple ways to provide the same (or a 
similar) clue to your Crew.
Even if you give the Crew a multitude of 
options to get past a roadblock, investiga-
tions can go wrong. The Players can fail 
every roll or miss every opportunity you 
give them and drain their Focus. That’s 
alright! Not even Sherlock Holmes was 
successful in every case, and noir protag-
onists like Philip Marlowe frequently ended 
Cyberware and 
Mysteries
It’s easy to think of cyber-
ware in terms of making 
a Crew’s life easier when 
investigating a situation. 
And it’s true that some, 
especially cyberoptics and 
audio, can be a big help. 
A detective with Olfactory 
Boost cyberware can 
smell lingering traces of 
a murderer’s perfume. 
An Edgerunner boasting 
a Chemical Analyzer 
can identify the specific 
poison used in the murder. 
Skill Chips can boost 
Criminology, Deduction, 
Tracking, or any Skill the 
Crew might need but lack.
When designing a 
mystery, remember the 
Edgerunners investigating 
it might have cyberware 
that transforms them into 
human-shaped crime labs 
and plan accordingly.


---

<!-- page 70 -->

69
DID SOMEONE SAY MURDER?
a day with a pile of disconnected information and no 
way to piece it together into something useful. When 
their Focus is depleted for the day, don’t feel you have 
to carry the Edgerunners over the speed bump. Let them 
rest, think it over, and come back fresh the next day. 
You can even slip a small side-mission between days 
to help clear their heads!
Of course, this may give the villains of the piece time 
to develop their scheme while the Edgerunners are 
mulling over their situation. Hiding evidence, silencing 
witnesses, or killing a new victim are just a few possible 
options and may add Obfuscations, Obstacles, and 
even new Clues to the mix.
The Denouement
This system can be used as nothing more than a series 
of Checks to keep track of progress, but it is more prop-
erly used as a springboard for roleplay. GMs can use 
it as a checklist if they like, but it works better when the 
Clues and Obstacles serve as a skeleton upon which 
they and the Players hang roleplaying opportunities. 
Describe the scene of the murder! Mention the smell of 
synthcoke and the odd weight of the footprints, as if the 
killer weighs at least five hundred pounds. Play out the 
interrogations, using the Checks to guide the direction 
of the “conversation.” Have fun with it!
One final note on mystery construction; not everything 
is epic. This goes double if you or the Players are new 
to the genre. Don’t leap headlong into a carefully con-
structed conspiracy as your first act; get your feet wet 
with a mugging, theft, or nice little murder. Bonus points 
if it happens to someone or somewhere that the Crew 
cares about. Making it personal isn’t just good inspiration 
for solving a mystery – it’s the Cyberpunk way.

Someone Stole it First!
Complexity: Average (50)
Goal: You’ve gained entry to a Midnight Market to 
steal a top-secret memory chip from the host’s safe, 
but when you got to the office someone beat you to it. 
▶ Possible Clues ◀
 
X Digital Scavenging
Check: Electronics/Security Tech DV13•Damage: 4d6
Obfuscation: 4•Focus Damage: 3d6
The event’s host uses a burner in addition to his Agent. 
Messages on there contain information about who 
wants the item.
 
X Forensics
Check: Criminology/Deduction DV13•Damage: 4d6
Obfuscation: 2•Focus Damage: 3d6
The safe in the office has been forced rather than 
opened using the combination.
 
X Gossip
Check: Conversation DV13•Damage: 2d6
Obfuscation: 2•Focus Damage: 1d6
Does the host have any enemies, who (aside from 
you) might want to steal from him? There are thirty 
guests but information starts repeating itself after six 
conversations.
 
X Interrogation
Check: Interrogation DV15•Damage: 3d6
Obfuscation: 2•Focus Damage: 2d6
Did any of the other attendees see anyone lurking sus-
piciously? There are thirty guests but information starts 
repeating itself after three conversations.
 
X Observation
Check: Human Perception DV17•Damage: 2d6
Obfuscation: 4•Focus Damage: 1d6
Someone at the event is explicitly trying not to draw the 
attention of security.
 
X Observation
Check: Wardrobe & Style DV13•Damage: 2d6
Obfuscation: 4•Focus Damage: 1d6
This event has a dress code. Who isn’t looking quite 
right?
 
X Research
Check: Library Search DV15•Damage: 2d6
Obfuscation: 2•Focus Damage: 1d6
Examine surveillance footage obtained from the 
Security Net Architecture.
 
X Tracking
Check: Tracking DV15•Damage: 2d6
Obfuscation: 4•Focus Damage: 1d6
The thief left distinctive boot marks on the office carpet, 
at odds with the overall theme of office-party-wear.
▶ Possible Obstacles ◀
Authority: You’re guests at an event and have no 
official standing to question attendees or influence 
security.
Digital: There is a camera in the office, but the Crew 
needs access to the NET Architecture or a console in 
the security office to watch it.
Distraction: The Midnight Market is still ongoing, and 
the Edgerunners must appear to be participating to 
avoid suspicion.
Red Herring: An attendee has stolen a parent’s 
invitation, and their nerves may be mistaken for guilt.
Territorial: The Crew cannot return to the crime scene 
without drawing unwanted attention from security guards.
Ticking Clock: The building is locked down and 
nobody has left yet. The doors will open when the 
Midnight Market ends.

72
YOUR NEW BEST FRIEND
T
he Next Gen of Cyberpets
t h e n e x t g e n o f c y b e r p e t s
by Ryder Lee, WNS News
Nostalgia is all the rage, and Biotechnica is delivering it 
in spades. Remember the cute and lovable Cyberpets® 
everyone had to get their hands on back in the 2020s? 
Well, if you don’t, now is your chance to discover what 
you missed! From lovable Forever Turtle™ to cute and 
fluffy Datarabbit™, Biotechnica’s newest line of cyber-
enhanced pets has you covered when it comes to 
finding the best possible companion for your protection 
and emotional fulfillment. A true pet for life. These are 
not just little toys you can turn on and watch squeak 
about with mechanical movement but real creatures 
who will provide you with love and attention. Once 
you let a Cyberpet® into your life, you’ll never return 
to the colorless, cold times when you were alone and 
bereft of their companionship. Biotechnica was nice 
enough to send over a Neon Newt™ for us to test out 
in the office.
Its bright shifting colors and ability to respond to com-
mands blew us away while its secret personal defense 
option lit up the room. This pocket-sized flamethrower 
could get anyone out of a pinch! “We wanted to launch 
Biotechnica into the future with a classic twist,” said 
Biotechnica lead bioengineer Dr. Talia Vex, “What 
better way to jump back into bioform than by bringing 
the best of modern cloning and cybertechnology to the 
Cyberpet® process?”
Since the fall of Adrek Robotics during the 4th 
Corporate War, the former leader in Cyberform 
models, Biotechnica has sought to fill a void in the 
robotics sector. And fill it they have! Not only have they 
revitalized their Cyberpets® line, but they have also 
begun rolling out bioform systems equal to or better 
than any security and utility drones by combining the 
ingenuity of human technology with the natural instincts 
of the animal kingdom.
We look forward to seeing what additional 
Cyberpets® Biotechnica brings to market in the near 
future! Currently, only eight models are available for 
purchase, leaving an almost infinite number of species, 
extinct or not, on tap to develop into new products!On 
a personal note, I asked Dr. Vex about the possibility of 
Cyberdinos, but she refused to answer. My inner child 
can still dream!
Update: Cyberpet® Issues
From: 	
Doctor_Talia_Vex
To: 	 	
Bioform_Project_Team
We have received reports of bioforms in the 
field acting erratic and/or showing signs of 
“cyberpsychotic breaks” despite our lab-
oratory testing proving this is impossi-
ble. While we should carefully monitor the 
situation, I have personally spoken to CEO 
Loggagia about the issue, and he agrees 
there is no need to pull our Cyberpet® line 
from the market. We are firmly of the opinion 
that any incidents are the result of misuse 
by the bioforms’ owners and not the result of 
a fault in our product. Additional warning 
labels have been added.
Keep up the good work as we save the planet!
Dr. Talia Vex,  
Lead Bioengineer, Cyberpet® line
K
eeping a Cyberpet
k
e
e
p
i
n
g
a c
y
b
e
r
p
e
t
Buying a Cyberpet isn’t cheap, and keeping one isn’t 
cheap, either.
First, you’ve got to find one – they’re a hot commod-
ity, so call a Fixer. Once you pony up the cash, you’ll 
need to keep your Cyberpet happy, which means 
buying it food, performing regular maintenance, and 
providing adequate space and housing. 
In game terms, that means paying for your new 
pet’s Lifestyle (see CP:R page 377) — that’s right, 
you’ve gotta pay monthly for its Lifestyle in addition 
to your own. There’s no cheating this cost. If you 
buy a Cyberrat in the middle of the month or during 
character generation, you’ve gotta pay for a month 
of full lifestyle. It is not prorated. Your pet also needs 
a place to sleep, and that counts toward the capacity 
of your Housing (see CP:R page 377). Mook-level 
pets do not take up much space and do not require an 
additional bed. Lieutenants take up one bed’s worth 
of space. If your new pet is a Mini-Boss it takes up two 
beds worth of space. For a Boss-level pet? Impressive, 
but that’s gonna count as 3. Also, check with your 
landlord. Even if they allow pets, don’t expect them 
to be chill with you keeping a bear in your apartment.
For more 
information 
about 
Biotechnica, see 
CP:R page 
268.

<!-- page 74 -->

73
YOUR NEW BEST FRIEND
T
raining a Cyberpet
t r a i n i n g a c y b e r p e t
Convincing a Cyberpet to obey requires 
actual skill on your part. If your Animal 
Handling Base (INT + Animal Handling) is 
less than 10, the animal will not obey your 
commands and will act as the GM decides.
If your Animal Handling Base is 10 or 
higher, your Cyberpet will love you, fight 
alongside you, and follow your commands 
to the best of its ability. However, this is 
contingent upon its care.
If you haven’t maintained your pet’s 
Lifestyle, if it hasn’t slept properly because 
of a lack of proper housing due to being 
crammed, or if you have mistreated your 
pet and betrayed its trust, the GM deter-
mines the pet’s behavior. It can run away, 
refuse to obey a command, or even turn on 
you in a rage. If such a situation arises, the 
GM may allow an Animal Handling Check 
to regain control of the Cyberpet, but this 
is a temporary measure. If you don’t invest 
time, energy, care, and money into your pet 
immediately, it will go rogue again.
C
ybering a Cyberpet
c y b e r i n g a c y b e r p e t
Implanting more cyberware into a 
Cyberpet is done with the same DVs as 
doing so in a human, but you’re not gonna 
be able to go into just any hospital and find 
someone who can help you put a cyberarm 
on a rat. You’ll need to find a Medtech with 
Science (Biology) 4 or higher and a spe-
cialized facility (as determined by the GM). 
Otherwise, the operation will fail and your 
Cyberpet won’t make it out alive.
Almost all cyberware designed for 
humans can be found in animal-compat-
ible configurations. Animal cyberware, 
however, is species-specific. You can’t take 
cyberware from one species and install it 
in another without a Tech Upgrade specif-
ically allowing cross-species compatibility. 
Even then, size matters. 
No matter how much you Tech Upgrade 
it, a bear’s cybereye won’t fit into a rat’s 
eye socket.
As for if your animal can be trained to 
understand and use the cyberware you’re 
chroming them the fuck up with, doing so 
takes a day of training and has an Animal 
Handling DV equal to Installation DV. 
Failure does not turn the Cyberpet against 
you, but the lack of adjustment means they 
will be unable to do more than eat and 
sleep until you try again and succeed.
Each Cyberpet comes with a base set of 
cyberware and has extra slots, allowing 
the owner to install additional cyberware 
after purchase. A Cyberpet cannot be Tech 
Upgraded to allow for additional slots but 
a Tech can Tech Upgrade one (and only 
one) piece of cyberware to take up 0 
slots in a Cyberpet. Uninstalling a piece of 
cyberware that was part of the pet’s base 
set does not give them extra slots but unin-
stalling cyberware that was installed after 
purchase does.
F
our Cyberpet Hooks
f o u r c y b e r p e t h o o k s
It Came From the Sewers: Rumors of a 
monster under Night City have everyone 
on high alert. 
Too Good to Be True: A shipping con-
tainer, thought to be full of electronic 
parts, is actually holding a Cyberbear.
Head of Security: A corporate insider 
within Biotechnica, feeling guilty about 
the deaths caused by the Cyberpet+ line, 
reaches out for protection as she leaves 
the company. An assassin is trying to 
silence her before she can blow the lid on 
the whole plot. 
Critter Combat: An underground 
Cyberpet fighting ring has been uncov-
ered. The gang responsible must be taken 
down. Caution: A dangerous number of 
Cyberwolves are aggressively loyal to 
their master, the ring leader Michele Vex.
Fixer 
Availability
Occasionally, cost in 
Cyberpunk RED doesn’t 
equate to how easy it is for 
a Fixer to get their paws on 
something.
An item or service might 
be pricey but easy to get 
or cheap but hard to get. 
In such cases, the cost in 
Eurodollars and the price 
category of the item can 
be different.
For example, purchas-
ing a Forever Turtle costs 
2,000eb, but GMs should 
treat it as Very Expensive 
for purposes of determining 
availability.

Combat Jaw•Grafted Muscle & Bone Lace•Neural Link w/ Chipware Socket & Pain Editor•TUp (+2 SP, Requires BODY 10) Subdermal Armor
As an unintended side effect of their creation, Cyberbears fight unlike any other creature on earth. While impossible to learn, 
duplicate, or imitate, their innate combat ability acts like a Martial Art with the following Special Moves.
Recovery
Requirement: The bear is knocked prone
Whenever the bear uses the Get Up Action, they can attempt to beat a DV13 with the Martial Arts Special Move Resolution. 
If they succeed, that Get Up Action didn’t cost an Action.
Cyberbear Three-Arm Strike
Requirement: The Cyberbear hits the same target with two Bear Claw attacks this turn.
Once per Turn when the bear fulfills this requirement, they can use the Martial Arts Special Move Resolution to attempt to 
beat a DV15. If they succeed, they may choose one of the following effects. However, should they fail, the tumor in their 
brain causes them to immediately suffer 5 damage directly to their HP.
•	 The bear can either grapple or end a grapple with their last hit  target. 
•	 The bear can attack their last hit target again with either a Bear Claw or Combat Jaw.
•	 The bear can cause their target to suffer a random Body Critical Injury (see CP:R page 187).
•	 The bear can reduce the SP of all their last hit target’s worn armor by 4.

of cyberware from Black Chrome, Interface RED

can activate a cloak that hides from all visual senses, 
including Low Light/Infrared/UV, unless the perceiver 
succeeds at a DV17 Perception Check. Activating the 
cloak does not require an Action. The cloak is deacti-
vated automatically if the user moves further than 2 m/
yds (1 square) within 3 seconds (1 Round). A second 
installation reduces the amount of time the user must 
remain still before the cloak can be activated to 30 
seconds (10 Rounds). A third installation reduces the 
time to 9 seconds (3 Rounds). Further installations do 
nothing.

Cyberarm Option. Requires 2 Option Slots. As 
an Action, a user can force a target with functional meat 
eyes or cyberoptics within 25 m/yds to make a DV15 Resist 
Torture/Drugs Check. No Check is required for this Action. 
If the target fails, they suffer the Damaged Eye Critical Injury 
for 1 minute.

option, protects against this effect.

ened to military standards. Cybereye and installed

 Screamsheet Generator

creating headlines for your campaign
BY ALEX RACINE

<!-- page 85 -->

84
SCREAMSHEET GENERATOR
Screamsheets! In-game, they befuddle, bemuse, and 
bedazzle the public of Night City by providing enter-
tainment, local gossip, and, on occasion, actual news! 
Out-of-game, they act as tools, allowing the GM to 
pass along important information, provide context, 
advance plot lines, show the consequences of actions, 
and explore the world outside of the campaign.
Of course, not everyone has a degree in journalism 
to help them write in-world articles. The Screamsheet 
Generator is here to help. Use it to randomly (or not 
so randomly) generate articles you can hand to your 
Players with just a bit of fill-in-the-blanks action, or use 
it to create base-level articles you can transform, twist, 
and change to fit the concept in your mind.
U
sing the Generator
u
s
i n
g
t
h
e
g
e
n
e
r
a
t o r
At its core, this generator consists of a collection of 
tables designed to allow you to create random content 
by rolling dice and combining the results. However, 
if you see an element that seems like a particularly 
good fit for your game, skip the die roll and grab it! 
The Screamsheet Generator is a tool to enhance your 
game. Use it however works best for you and your 
Players!
The Screamsheet Generator works by directing you 
through a collection of tables. At each step, you will 
select a piece of content. Be sure to copy these down as 
you go. Then, complete any fillable fields, denoted by 
brackets (Example: [Name 1]), polish any rough areas, 
and create a handout to share with your group!
Here’s the process.
Step 1: Type of Article
The first thing you’ll select is the type of article you’re 
creating. Do you want a news flash? An entertainment 
piece? A help wanted ad? The format you choose will 
determine which content tables you use to create the 
article. Use Table A to select the type of article you 
want to create.
Step 2: Content Paragraphs
There are different sets of content tables for each type 
of article listed in Table A. Once you’ve selected the 
type of article you’re creating, turn to the relevant 
content tables and roll once on each table to select 
your article’s intro, body, and conclusion. Some para-
graphs may work better together than others, so don’t 
hesitate to reroll or rewrite some of the text to create a 
better final article.
To make these screamsheets modular, proper nouns 
and key details have been left for the user to fill in. 
These fillable fields are in brackets (Ex: [Name 1]) and 
will be dealt with during Step 4.
Step 3: Headline
Readers begin an article by reading the headline but 
most journalists pick their headline after the article is 
written. This is because, in reality, headlines have more 
to do with how an incident resolves than with how it 
begins. The headline is, therefore, tied to the conclusion 
paragraph. Once you’ve selected a conclusion, the 
instructions in the generator will point you to a choice 
of headlines. 
Step 4: Filling in the Details
Now it’s time to fill in all the blanks - those fields in 
brackets in your article. You can select people, places, 
or things from one of the many Cyberpunk RED 
books and DLCs, grab a proper noun from your own 
campaign, or make up something on the spot! This is 
where you personalize the article to fit your table. 
Where a fillable field indicates a part of speech (Ex: 
“[Adjective]”), you can either choose a word yourself, 
or, for a bit of levity, you can ask your players to supply 
the missing word as if the article was part of an impro-
visational party game.
Step 5: Polish
You’ve selected your content paragraphs. You’ve 
chosen a headline. You’ve filled in any missing details. 
The last step is to read your article from start to finish to 
make sure it makes sense. 
It’s important to give each article a final read from 
start to finish. While the pieces of each style of article 
are designed to fit together, the random nature of 
this generator means that sometimes it may generate 
something that doesn’t make a lot of sense. Remember 
that the paragraphs provided here are just building 
blocks to enhance your game. Don’t hesitate to reroll 
or replace any part of the article that doesn’t fit well, or 
that doesn’t fit the narrative you’re trying to tell!

SCREAMSHEET GENERATOR
H
ow it works: an example
h o w i t w o r k s a n e x a m p l e
Kerry, the GM, decides they’d like to gen-
erate a screamsheet article to add some 
depth to their game. 
Kerry wants to emphasize that, while the 
players have been focusing on their own 
missions, bad things have been happening 
elsewhere in the city, so they decide to 
skip rolling on Table A and use the “News 
Flash” tables to generate their article.
Kerry rolls a d6 on each content table 
to randomly choose the pieces of their 
article. They roll three times: once for the 
intro, once for the body, and once for the 
conclusion. 
Kerry rolls a 1, a 2, and a 6, generating 
an article that looks like this ...
Intro: As our regular readers may 
recall, [Name 1], a prominent [Relationship 
Type] of [Group 1], was reported missing 
[Number] weeks ago after the [Incident] at 
[Location]. [Group 1] has offered a reward 
for any information leading to [Name 1]’s 
safe return, but little progress has been 
made since their disappearance.
Body: The situation got violent last 
night. Sources report that after an intense 
firefight with [Group 2], [Group 1] agents 
were seen hurrying a disheveled [Job Title] 
named [Name 1] out of the area. Our 
reporters attempted to follow the trail but 
lost track of the crew amid the busy streets.
Conclusion: Meanwhile, the situation 
went from bad to worse for locals when 
a [Adjective] drone stored somewhere 
in [Group 2]’s facility was activated. The 
machine rampaged through the area, 
crushing vehicles and bystanders caught in 
its path and bringing down the front wall of 
a nearby building before local lawmen were 
able to neutralize it.
When asked for comment, [Group 2]’s 
spokesperson merely said, “With innova-
tion comes risk. We can only hope that the 
people of Night City realize how lucky 
they are to be on the cutting edge of such 
marvelous advancements.”
[Group 2] has offered to compensate the 
victims of this most recent mishap, provided 
they can document their losses.
. . .
These pieces of text do make a coherent 
story, but beginning by talking about a 
missing person and ending with a para-
graph about a rampaging robot seems a 
little disconnected to Kerry. 
They decide to reroll the first paragraph. 
This time they get a 3, directing them to a 
new intro ...
New Intro: “We never could have 
planned for this,” [Group 1]’s spokesper-
son commented in the wake of the recent 
disaster.
. . .
Kerry likes this intro better but decides 
it would make more sense for the group 
with the rampaging drone to be issuing the 
statement. They keep this intro but change 
[Group 1] to [Group 2] here. Kerry also 
notices that the transition from the intro to 
the body feels awkward, so they decide to 
remove the first sentence of the body.
Next Kerry needs a headline. They look 
at the note at the end of their conclusion 
paragraph, which reads, “NF4.” 
If they had chosen a different conclusion 
paragraph, Kerry might have been directed 
to choose between several different head-
lines, but this one is pretty straightforward. 
Kerry looks at the headlines for the “News 
Flash” articles and finds NF4 ...
Stolen Drone Rampages Through 
[Location (Neighborhood)]!
Kerry tweaks the headline to be more 
sensational, then adds it to the top of their 
article, leaving them with something that 
looks like this ...

Rogue Drone Rampages  
Through Woodland Park!
“We never could have planned for this,” Dynalar’s spokes-
person commented in the wake of the recent disaster.
The situation got violent last night. Sources report that 
after an intense firefight with Dynalar, Team Monster 
agents were seen hurrying a disheveled Senior Dynalar 
Engineer named Peter Paulmary out of the area. Our 
reporters attempted to follow the trail but lost track of 
the crew amid the busy streets.
Meanwhile, the situation went from bad to worse for 
locals when a Construction drone stored somewhere 
in Dynalar’s facility was activated. The machine 
rampaged through the area, crushing vehicles and 
bystanders caught in its path and bringing down the 
front wall of a nearby building before MAX-TAC was 
able to neutralize it.
When asked for comment, Dynalar’s spokesperson 
merely said, “With innovation comes risk. We can only 
hope that the people of Night City realize how lucky 
they are to be on the cutting edge of such marvelous 
advancements.”
Dynalar has offered to compensate the victims of this most 
recent mishap, provided they can document their losses.

drone stored somewhere in [Group 2]’s hideout was activated. The machine 
rampaged through the area, crushing vehicles and bystanders caught in its path 
and bringing down the front wall of a nearby building before [Group 3] was 
able to neutralize it. 
When asked for comment, [Group 2]’s spokesperson merely said, “With 
innovation comes risk. We can only hope that the people of Night City realize 
how lucky they are to be on the cutting edge of such marvelous advancements.”
[Group 2] has offered to compensate the victims of this most recent mishap, 
provided they can properly document their losses and sign appropriate legal 
waivers.

Once you’ve finished generating and tweaking your Screamsheet set it up to present to your Players. You can keep 
it low key and simple by placing the article(s) in a document and handing it over in your preferred format, but if you 
want to go the extra mile, download the Screamsheet Generator Template Pack. The Pack contains print-ready JPG 
images featuring two of Night City’s most popular Screamsheet outlets: Night City Today News (a more formal and 
traditional news source) and The Augmented Optic (which reads more like a group of somewhat informative blog 
posts). You can pop the images into a word processor or image manipulation program and format your articles 
over the top before presenting your unique Screamsheet to your Crew on game night.
We recommend ½ inch (1.27 cm) margins on the left, right, and bottom. The gutters between the text and the 
header and between the columns should be ¼ inch (.635 cm). To match our fonts, follow these guidelines.
 
X Night City Today News
We use a font named Futura FT specifically, but any 
Futura should do the job.
Headline Font: Futura Bold (14 point, 13.5 leading)
Byline Font: Futura Oblique (8 point, 8 leading))
Body Text Font: Futura (11 point, 12.5 leading)
Link Font: Futura Bold followed by Futura (10 point, 
6 leading). Color is #3F4791
 
X Augmented Optic
We use a font named Video for the Augmented Optic, 
but any 80s CRT computer screen-style font will do.
Headline Font: Video Bold (12 point, 13.5 leading)
Byline Font: Video (8 point, 4 point leading)
Body Text Font: Video (10 point, 14 point leading)
Link Font: None. The Augmented Optic is an 
extremely low effort publication.

THE 12 DAYS OF REDMAS
I saw Krampus 
I saw Krampus flatlined
flatlined 
by S.A.N.T.A’s plow, while 
by S.A.N.T.A’s plow, while 
riding through the Combat 
riding through the Combat 
Zone 
Zone last
last night.
 night.
— 
— Wheelie
Wheelie
“There isn’t room in Night City for two Christmas 
themed Cyberpunk mascots,” the cloven-hooved borg, 
Krampus, heard from a speaker taped to a brick wall. 
The street lights flickered out. With one simple netrun, 
S.A.N.T.A. had transformed the street into his own 
private Monster Fight Club.
In the darkness, 500 m/yds away, at the other end of 
a custom red and white spiral fluted barrel, a man with 
a voluminous white beard chambered an experimental 
.50 BMG EMP round he had purchased from a Tech 
known to the street as Never Naughty Nedd. Each 
bullet ran a jolly 50eb (Costly), but the big man had 
enough in his sack to afford it. His cybereyes flashed 
blue with night vision, bringing his target into focus at 
the other end of his ACOG scope. He took two deep 
breaths, hyperoxygenating, growling with each exhale. 
“Ho... Ho…. “ 
He pulled the trigger. “Ho.” 
The bullet ripped through Krampus’s Cyberjaw, show-
ering the wall behind him in stainless steel teeth. An 
electromagnetic pulse dashed through the borg like a 
comet, leaving the inferior Cyberpunk holiday mascot 
immobilized in the snow.
Slinging the rifle onto his back, the jolly old body-
sculpted elf saddled his custom Diego Motors Range 
Trike and deployed a Tup’d Combat Plow airbrushed 
with an image of a 12-pointed reindeer by the Jodes. 
The headlamp glowed RED. The big man flicked the 
NOS switch and the scent of rubber filled the street. 
At 200 miles per hour (322 kph), as young Wheelie 
looked on aghast, S.A.N.T.A. taught Krampus the true 
meaning of Christmas.
Stepping down from his sleigh onto the broken body 
of the borg, the electrical wires spilling from Krampus’ 
ripped-open innards reminded S.A.N.T.A. of a pump-
kin-head he had flatlined eighty-four days prior; a kill 
he had forgotten to record. With a night-black leather 
gloved hand, he reached inside the mess and ripped 
out a wet Biopod, which he set gently on the ground. 
Backing up, he crushed the cybernetic chamber, brain 
and choice bits and all, underneath Rudolph’s Heavy 
Chassis like an old Christmas ornament at a recycling 
plant. 
The old Turk pulled a list from his pocket. He checked 
it off. Twice.
“That’s the last of them, Nikola. For now.” he chuck-
led to himself. “Time to get going. You’ve got a tight 
schedule to keep. So many Edgerunners have been so 
good this year.”
•••
Just before midnight 12/24 at the Short Circuit, thirty 
or so Techs in Santa outfits burst out into The Street 
when they heard a jolly voice call, “Merry Christmas 
to all, no matter the creed! Information, and people, 
deserve to be free!”
Yet they only found a speaker hanging from a 
streetlamp like a bauble from a tree. Going back inside, 
they heard the server fans spinning and spinning, 
overheating with joy! They saw the LED status lights 
flashing, blinking merry Cyberpunk RED and bright 
v3 Green!
And just like that, the Library was once again over-
flowing with fresh schematics to print, modify, and sell 
freely. Truly, it was a REDMas miracle!
 
X Centurion Essentials Thermal Dagger

cutting through tough plastic is a hot knife. Now, you 
can use that same technology for your professional 
organic liquidation needs with the Centurion Essentials 
Thermal Dagger. 
For an extra 10eb we’ll even engrave your name 
onto the blade so you can brand it into your enemy’s 
flesh.
An Excellent Quality Exotic Medium Melee Weapon 
that can be concealed. Anyone or anything hit by the 
Thermal Dagger is now Strongly On Fire (see CP:R 
page 180).

by a community of audio enthusiast Techs, Smart Ears 
can be printed in hundreds of sizes and shapes to 
match your needs and aesthetic.
Smart Ears come with a non-removable Radio Scanner/
Music Player installed and 2 additional Option Slots for 
Cyberaudio Options. When worn, Smart Ears give the user 
access to the benefits of these options. Installing or uninstall-
ing a Cyberaudio Option into Smart Ears takes one hour. 
You can only wear one set of Smart Ears at a time.

Solo of Fortune 2045
Mr. Editor
By John “The Hoss” Havel Junior
Effective immediately, stop buying Solo of Fortune if you’ve never seen combat. This magazine isn’t for you. Solo 
of Fortune is for professionals  who put food on the table with hard-earned skills. It’s where we publish letters from 
the who’s who of modern warriors. It’s a place of honor and respect for those who wield the power to dominate the 
battlefield. It isn’t a zoo window from which to gawk slackjawed at the dangerous life of a mercenary. It’s for Solos, by 
Solos. Attempting to duplicate what you read in this magazine will get you killed, likely by a reader of this magazine. 
So piss off, poser. This includes the staff of Puddleforge Games. Go find “gritty” material for your Cyborg Anarchist 
books somewhere else. Up your own ass, preferably.
GM WARNING
This content is above power level of most Street Level campaigns. If you allow your Players to take items from Solo of 
Fortune 2045, you are increasing the power and/or complexity level of the campaign from street level to one more 
suited to telling mercenary stories. If you’re aiming for street level, you may want to restrict these items in your campaign to 
a one-off storyline, or even disallow them entirely. You’ll certainly need to field more difficult Hardened threats to challenge 
a party with these items. See Hardened DLC series for more information.
Hot Tip
To Harden an NPC in a hurry, increase all of attacking and defending Skills by 2, and Tech Upgrade their armor to either 
reduce all of its armor penalties by 1 or increase SP by 1. Your Hardened NPCs shouldn’t have Poor Quality items unless 
they’re Mooks.
For Players
If you equip items from Solo of Fortune 2045, your Edgerunner is immediately considered “Hardened”, meaning your 
GM will field Hardened threats. The items in this DLC are a ticket straight to a tougher game.
It’s All Optional!
Solo of Fortune 2045 adds new rules, new gear, and new complications. It has the potential of increasing the crunch 
level of your game, so remember, this is all optional. If it bogs down your game and dampens the fun, put it aside and try 
again later. This is especially true for newer  gamers. We recommend trying out the basics of Cyberpunk RED before 
layering on new rules.

Introducing Mercenary Level
What is the Mercenary Level?
Mercenary Level takes the action beyond The Street by 
focusing on tactical combat and free-form missions. It poses 
an increased challenge, but offers increased rewards. At 
the Street Level, Edgerunners are often hired to be criminals 
and finish jobs. At the Mercenary Level, Edgerunners are 
hired to complete objectives in intense missions.
How does a Mercenary Level Campaign 
differ from a Street Level Campaign?
Mercenary gameplay focuses on missions that the Crew is 
tasked to undertake, where planning and tactical gameplay 
are required from the start in order to succeed. The focus of 
a Mercenary Campaign shifts away from heists, which are 
better played at Street Level, and towards high-stakes combat 
encounters. The personality of the Cyberpunk world remains 
the same, but the ability to affect change within it is drastically 
changed. A successful mission at the Mercenary Level can 
easily tip the balance of power in a region, toppling factions 
and changing the status quo for better or worse.
Do all Roles fit in the  
Mercenary Level of play?
Yes, but you might need to be flexible, and even creative 
to explain their character’s motivations for being there. The 
GM will have to be similarly flexible to explain how some 
Role Abilities might work in distant lands. Lawmen and 
Execs may have to work with local factions, for example. 
Just as in Street Level Campaigns, it’s the responsibility of 
everyone at the table to work together to tell the story. 
Mercenary Morality
There’s room for moral choices in gigs on The Street. Crews 
can pick local jobs and work against the rich and powerful 
instead of for them. Mercenary Level doesn’t offer the same 
level of flexibility. Most merc work is funded by corporations, 
governments, or the otherwise extremely wealthy. When you 
go merc, you risk selling your soul to the highest bidder.
Mercenary Level Balancing
Everything at Mercenary Level is Hardened – for more 
information on Hardened threats, see the Hardened 
DLC series. Beyond that, at the Mercenary Level, balance 
is fundamentally different. Instead of being balanced 
around Edgerunner count, at the Mercenary Level, the 
opposition the crew faces is determined entirely by the 
situation they find themselves in. Military bases don’t 
magically contain fewer people because you’ve brought 
a small squad to assault them. 
The quality of enemies the crew faces increases at the 
Mercenary Level as well. Hardened Lieutenants represent 
the average combatant you’ll face at the Mercenary 
Level. Hardened Mooks are used sparingly to depict 
trainees and new hires. Hardened Minibosses and Bosses 
are saved for challenging encounters, climactic moments, 
and when encountering fierce resistance. The quality of 
the equipment, armor, and weapons your enemies equip 
themselves with also increases dramatically. The GM has 
full access to the weapons and items in this book. Expect 
the opposition to be equipped with military-grade gear.
As an additional bit of spice, at the Mercenary Level, the 
GM is encouraged to field Complete Package characters, 
complete with Role Abilities, to represent rival crews, with 
some further IP spent to juice them to the level of the crew. 
To underscore their equality to the players, Rival edgerunn-
ners included in a Mercenary Mission can even use LUCK. 
These rival crews are usually hired to stop or interfere 
with your Crew’s ability to complete their Mission, or even 
to undercut them and steal the treasure/data/etc, right 
out from under them. They are a powerful way to intro-
duce recurring NPCs that can show up down the road as 
rivals, or maybe even to show up as an allies or just as 
a social encounter in a bar tucked away in a forgotten 
corner of the world.
Finally, the Mercenary Level of play is not balanced 
around looting everything you see at the end of your 
mission. Your objective is the mission, not stripping the

Solo of Fortune 2045
enemy for parts. Besides, the spoils of war belong to your 
employer, not you. Have your fun on the battlefield, but 
you will be expected to check your gear and your loot 
into the armory when you return to base.
Mercenary Resources & Mission Rewards
Payouts from Mercenary Missions are higher than those from 
Street Level jobs, and come with an Operational Budget, 
which is given at the start of the job. The Crew can’t keep 
anything that they buy with this Operational Budget after 
the Mission, including cyberware and upgrades for vehi-
cles, weapons, and ACPAs. Anything they don’t spend goes 
away. Your employer might have a Fixer to help source gear, 
but don’t be afraid to call on your own contacts, too.
Many Missions take place far away from the Crew’s 
home stomping grounds. While on such a Mission, char-
acters do not need to pay rent, and once they’re on site, 
the crew is typically given access to a Headquarters (See 
No Place Like Home DLC) until the job is complete. It’s 
always the GM’s call if travel is handwaved or a separate 
session’s worth of unexpected adventure. 
The Fixer’s Role Ability works for negotiating increased 
pay for Missions the same way it does on Jobs. IP rewards 
remain the same, except that every Mercenary Mission 
you survive counts as at least a successful Mission for the 
purpose of assigning Group IP.
Mission Parameters
Gamemasters, use the following examples to develop 
your Mercenary Missions.
 
X “Easy” Mission	
Organized and armed resistance is expected. No mission 
can truly be considered easy. 
“Destroy a convoy before it reaches a manufacturing 
facility. Eliminate all targets except the driver of the third 
car. An ACPA pilot, callsign Tyrant Wing, will attempt to 
intervene. Kill them.”	
Operational Budget (per person): 1,000 to 5,000eb
Mission Pay (per person): 3,000 to 4,000eb
 
X Typical Mission	
The enemy force is entrenched and far outnumbers our 
own, but the Crew has certain tactical advantages that 
will level the playing field. 
“Participate in a night raid of a suspected resistance base 
in the mountains. Capture a member of their leadership 
to stand trial, we don’t care which one. We expect to 
encounter heavy resistance if reinforcements have time 
to scramble, so act quickly. Should our intel be faulty and 
the facility turn out to be not resistance-allied, leave no 
witnesses.”
Operational Budget (per person): 2,000 to 7,000eb
Mission Pay (per person): 4,000 to 7,000eb	
 
X Dangerous Mission	
The enemy outnumbers the Crew, outclasses them, 
outguns them. Outmaneuvered, they grasp desperately 
at a slim chance of victory.	
“Destroy Petrochem’s three newly established research 
bases in the Blue Mountains within a two week time 
period. Do not wear or carry identifiers. If you are cap-
tured, we will deny all involvement.”
Operational Budget (per person): 10,0000 to 30,000eb
Mission Pay (per person): 6,000 to 9,000eb

Under Extreme Circumstances
By “Captain” Olav “The Mechanic”
The Neo-Soviet air of the Barents Sea is icy in my lungs. 
I’m on an independently operated fishing and part time 
mine-sweeping boat stuffed full of Norwegian-born 
sailors. I am thousands of miles from land, and thousands 
of eurobucks in the hole in knockout gas for the vessel’s 
crew, who are now sleeping soundly. I had put them 
under the impression I was chartering their fine vessel to 
do corporate espionage, to take some pictures of the oil 
rig poking over the horizon. The truth is that I’m here to 
take pictures with a bit more flash than they’re used to. If 
I time this operation wrong, the volley of return fire won’t 
be pretty, and a few of them might not wake up. The com-
puterized aiming systems of the six Archimedes briefcase 
missiles I’ve laid out over every horizontal surface of the 
deck click, beep, and go green-green. It was hard enough 
to get this close without alerting SovOil’s many ears in the 
area. There are a couple of ways this can go down, but 
I’m very sure I’m never getting another shot at this, and 
furthermore, I doubt anyone ever will. If SovOil’s satellites 
were still up and properly aligned like they were twenty 
years ago, I wouldn’t even be here. Times have changed. 
Plenty of these corporation’s eyes are still blind from injuries 
sustained during the war. Weakness like this simply wasn’t 
tolerated. I throw up a jammer and cover the gear with a 
ghostsuit tarp, waiting for a moment of tactical advantage. 
I look out to the ocean. There’s a storm coming in from 
the Arctic Circle, which should hit us in twenty minutes. 
That’s good for the job, but introduces challenges. I’m 
serviceable behind the wheel of a ship, but an arctic 
storm easily stretches my abilities. I realize I’m going to 
have to wake one of the crew up. I pull the ripcord on one 
of my insurance policies, injecting Surge via Bodyweight 
Autoinjector. I won’t have time to rest tonight. Looking 
over the sleeping crew, I choose a fourth mate, a good 
mix of talent and, in case things go poorly, a comparative 
lack of attachment to the rest of the crew. After dragging 
his body above deck, I lay him down on the floor of the 
helm room and  uninstall his jawbone radio communica-
tor unit. I’m again reminded that a tool hand is the most 
profitable cyberware a merc can install. I handcuff him 
to the wheel. Rainfall begins to hit the deck. The storm is 
rolling in.
Weapons check. Left side, Magnum Opus Hellbringer, 
Short Barrel, Midnight Arms Explosive Ammunition. 
Function, live. Left side, Kendachi Mono-Guard. Live. 
Right side, Magnum Opus Hellbringer, Long Barrel. 
Function, live. Emplacement, Arasaka WSSA Sniper 
System, Long Barrel, Custom Scope. Function, live. I dial in 
the scope for potential chase boats. There’s little chance 
I’m hitting anything at this distance in storm conditions, but 
at least hostiles won’t be either. SovOil likely won’t even 
be able to get boats in the water - those will be the first 
Archimedes targets.
Time to wake up my wheel man. “Проснуться!” My 
cyberhand holds their mouth closed until they calm 
down. They choose to comply, making my job much 
easier. The rain is freezing cold. I take my other hand 
off my Hellbringer and relay instructions to the pacified 
fisherman.
Time to start the fireworks. I kill the jammer and remove 
the tarps. The Archimedes systems respond, clicking, 
pre-arming, lighting green. I start with the leftmost brief-
case missile. Three seconds to arm. Fire. A good arc 
lands a clean impact on the oil rig, felling the radio tower, 
I look out to the 
ocean. There’s a 
storm coming in from 
the Arctic Circle, 
which should hit us 
in twenty minutes. 
That’s good for the 
job, but introduces 
challenges.

My pain editor does its job.
Through the Arctic storm, my Hellbringer was persua-
sive enough to prevent mutiny once the crew began to 
wake up. I spent the night awake from the Surge. One-
armed, with a pistol in hand, I daresay I felt a bit like a 
pirate.
When we docked in Vardø, my client was quick to 
transfer the money, and the fishermen were happy to see 
me gone. I’m just happy I didn’t have to kill any of them. 
I wasn’t getting paid enough for that.
The incoming rocket 
explodes in the air. 
Skeet shooting my 
50 cal rips my left 
shoulder out of 
its socket, but I’m 
alive.
While the heat signature of the incoming missile is easy 
enough to lock on to, my missile isn’t swift enough to inter-
cept it - too much payload weighing it down. I drop the 
controls and shoulder my sniper rifle with just enough time 
to whip the most expensive piece of my kit skyward and 
take a shot. The incoming rocket explodes in the air. Skeet 
shooting my 50 cal rips my left shoulder out of its socket, 
but I’m alive. I stumble back to the briefcase to regain 
control of my missile, white-knuckling the joystick to pull 
it down into a hit on the upper deck. The experimental 
pilebunker bores down into the rig’s lower accommoda-
tion deck, exploding with a heavy shockwave. I load the 
final missile into the archimedes, sending it toward the 
crack in the southwestern support column. It lands square

All New Attachments
The professional mercenary needs the tools of their job to 
be tuned exactly to their specifications. With these new 
attachments, you’ll be ready for the Mercenary Level 
Missions that await you.
 
X Ammunition Compatibility Internals
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All ranged weapons that fire bullets or shells, 
except flamethrowers.
Forcing something where it’s not meant to be isn’t always 
a good idea, and hey, maybe there was a reason that 
you weren’t supposed to chamber incendiary ammo in 
your plastic rifle. Stopping ideas like these from blowing 
up in your face is what gunsmithing wisdom and expe-
rience is for. Do it right, and things might end up better 
than you expected.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot 
to install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. 
Attaching this attachment requires a DV17 
Weaponstech Skill Check. Only one Ammunition 
Compatibility Internals may be installed. Once 
installed, this attachment may not be uninstalled. This 
weapon can now fire all Non-Basic Ammunition of any 
Ammunition category (Heavy Pistol, Assault Rifle, etc.) 
it could fire before installation.
 
X Reinforced String
Cost: 250eb (Expensive)
Eligible: Bows, Crossbows
Switching to a less forgiving string allows you to put sig-
nificantly more power behind each arrow. Kits can be 
purchased for the task.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Attaching 
this attachment requires a DV17 Archery or Weaponstech 
Skill Check. Only one Reinforced String may be installed. 
Once installed, this attachment can not be uninstalled. 
This weapon’s damage increases by 1d6 to a maximum 
of 5d6 when its user is not in a Kyudo Stance (see 
Interface RED vol 4 page 80). Unless installed in 
cyberware, this weapon must be fired with two hands, 
even if it was previously a one-handed weapon.
 
X Sniper Rifle Rechamber
Cost: 300eb (Expensive)
Eligible: Sniper Rifles
Rechambering a high caliber caseless weapon to an even 
higher caliber requires much more than drilling a wider 
hole. Headspace must be considered, and in many cases, 
the weapon’s receiver needs extensive modification. 
Luckily, they sell kits for that.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Attaching 
a Sniper Rifle Rechamber requires a DV17 Weaponstech 
Skill Check. Only one Sniper Rifle Rechamber may be 
installed. Once installed, this attachment can not be 
uninstalled. This weapon’s damage increases by 1d6, to 
a maximum of 6d6.
 
X Compatibility Rail
Cost: 50eb (Costly)
Eligible: All Exotic Ranged Weapons
Attaching an external scope rail to an unusual or unique 
weapon design requires certain considerations. The end 
result won’t always look good, but with enough gunsmith-
ing elbow grease, it’ll work.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Attaching 
this attachment requires a DV17 Weaponstech Skill 
Check. Only one Compatibility Rail may be installed at 
a time. This weapon gains an Attachment Slot that can 
only be used to install Scope attachments.

X Range Table Modification
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All Ranged Weapons, Individual eligibility by 
weapon category.
Nothing stock is perfect, and that’s why God invented 
barrel extensions. Screw one on and you’ve got a 
weapon designed to work at the range you want to be 
fighting in. These have been around for so long that you 
don’t need a lick of talent to get one running on your 
favorite side piece. Just remember to carry a backup in 
case the battlefield isn’t the exact size you preordained 
it to be.
This attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and may be installed in Exotic Weapons. Only 
one Range Table Modification may be installed at a time. 
While installed, the Range Table the weapon fires on is 
changed to match that of the Modification. The weapon’s 
damage, ammunition types, and other particulars are 
unaffected.

PRINT PROGRAM
Midnight Arms is happy to announce the launch of the Midnight Arms Presents program.  
Through this program’s first wave, Midnight Arms is now the exclusively authorized  
manufacturer/dealer in the Free State of Northern California for independent weapon designers  
Koshiro Yuzu, BlueRaven, and Aleksandr Petrov. 
Don’t trust your personal safety to an unauthorized techprint by an unlicensed dealer!  
Midnight Arms techprinted products come with the satisfaction of knowing that your purchase 
supports the weapon’s designer, who in turn guarantees its quality by trusting only Midnight Arms!
Lydia says:
“I only use designer 
weapons when I’m out 
on an op! If you want 
to share a campsite 
with me, you’ll use 
designer weapons, 
too!”
To apply for region exclusive Midnight Arms buyer 
account please contact Midnight Arms distribution 
via this Data Pool Link.

ACPAs
