AutoFIRE Heaven, Bullet Hell
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Attaching 
this attachment requires a DV17 Weaponstech Skill 
Check. Only one Shotgun Automatic Control Group may 
be installed. 
While loaded with slugs, this weapon gains Autofire 
(Machine Pistol 3), or Autofire (Machine Pistol 4) if the 
weapon is Excellent Quality or was already capable of 
Autofire, which this Autofire replaces. This weapon con-
sumes 4 slugs when fired in Autofire mode.
While loaded with shells, when using the Shotgun Shell 
alternate firing mode the user can choose to consume 4 
shotgun shells instead of 1 and attack using the Autofire 
Skill instead of the Shoulder Arms Skill. Anyone attempt-
ing to dodge this attack suffers a -3 penalty to their Skill 
Check. Damage is still dealt as normal for a Shotgun 
Shell.
 
X SMG Cyclic Internals
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All ranged weapons with Autofire (SMG 3)
Improving the cyclic rate of an SMG requires a familiar-
ity with weapons that exceeds that of a typical shooter. 
With enough knowledge, and the right kit of precision 
parts, you can manage to squeeze more power out of 
the weapon.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Attaching 
this attachment requires a DV17 Weaponstech skill check. 
Only one SMG Cyclic Internals may be installed. When 
installed, choose to replace the weapon’s Autofire (SMG 
3) with either Autofire (SMG 4) or (Machine Pistol 4).
New Weapon Category, New Autofire
Guide to reading the new Weapon Listings
In Solo of Fortune 2045, We’re trying a new method 
of communicating the features of a weapon that’s easier 
to read and reference at your game table.
Machine Guns are a new weapon category with no 
“generic” version — each weapon is unique, though all 
use Assault Rifle ammunition unless otherwise stated.
For attachment compatibility purposes, Machine Guns 
will accept any attachments as if they were classified 
as an Assault Rifle. As part of this new category, we’ve 
updated two guns from the Cyberpunk RED core rule-
book with new variants. 
Alongside Machine Guns, we’re also introducing two 
new types of autofire: Machine Gun and Machine Pistol. 
Each has their own row on the new Expanded Autofire 
Table (see page 113). 
And now, without further adieu, your big guns.
Autofire Attachments
 
X Pistol Autosear
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All Pistols except those capable of firing only 
slugs, shells, or arrows.
A simple switch, easily installed, can make most handguns 
capable of automatic fire.
This Attachment doesn’t require an Attachment Slot to 
install and can be installed in Exotic Weapons. Only one 
Pistol Autosear may be installed. This weapon gains the 
Autofire (Machine Pistol 3) and Suppressive Fire firing 
modes. If installed into an Excellent Quality pistol or 
a pistol already capable of Autofire, it grants Autofire 
(Machine Pistol 4), which replaces any existing Autofire 
it had access to.
 
X Shotgun Automatic Control Group
Cost: 100eb (Premium)
Eligible: All Weapons capable of firing slugs and/or 
shells.
Making a shotgun capable of automatic fire is much more 
complicated than a handgun, requiring a modification to the 
weapon’s fire control group, which requires a skilled hand.

Weapon Name: The name of the weapon.
Cost: The cost of the weapon, followed by its availability.
Flavor Text: In-world lore and a description of the 
weapon.
Classification: Weapon Quality and the class of the 
weapon for ammunition and attachment compatibility.
Skill: This is the skill the weapon is fired with in Single Shot 
(if applicable).
Range Table: This is the Range Table this weapon is fired 
with in Single Shot (if applicable).
Damage: This is the damage dealt by this weapon.
ROF: This is the rate of fire of the weapon.
Hands: The number of hands the weapon is fired with.
Capacity: The amount of ammunition held by this 
weapon.
Special Firing Modes: This section lists any Special 
Firing Modes the weapon has.
Concealable: Whether or not the weapon is concealable.
Open Slots: This is the number of open attachment slots 
remaining on the weapon.
Attachments: These attachments come already installed 
in the weapon.
Special Features: Any special features the weapon has.
 
X Arasaka Neo Rapid Assault 16
Cost: 1,600eb (Very Expensive)
Years after receiving backlash from their loyal customer 
base for their “semi-auto disrespect” of the beloved Rapid 
Assault 12, Arasaka has begun anew with the Neo Rapid 
Assault 16. Its high price tag remains a stretch for aging 
fans of the Rapid Assault 12, and may doom its future as 
a viable mass-market shotgun.

A high capacity SMG made as a larger caliber version 
of the popular Minami 10 for rare security forces where 
stealth and concealability are not important. While 
the Takanami became immediately popular inside the 
company, sales to outside firms remain poor.

a backpack through a water-tight tube. All this firepower 
comes at the cost of considerable weight and the expense of 
ammunition efficiency, although MetaCorp didn’t design it for 
anyone who would consider either of these a dealbreaker.

X Burrowing Ammunition

manently. The DV to perform a Quick Fix or Treatment on a

X Explosive Ammunition
Cost: 100eb (Premium) for 10 
Ammo Types Available: Arrows, Bullets, and Slugs.
KaBOOM! This ammunition will send body parts flying clean 
off into the next district over when you sweep your street. It’s 
a whole new way to celebrate the fourth of July every single 
day of the year. It even comes in arrows, too! Isn’t that neat?
This ammunition can not be used to make an Aimed Shot. 
Attacks made with this ammunition are at a -2 penalty. 
When using this ammunition, 5’s are treated as 6’s on 
your damage dice solely for the purpose of determining 
if your attack causes a Critical Injury.
 
X High Precision Ammunition
Cost: 100eb (Premium) for 10

hardly see these things come out the barrel, they’re going 
so fast. Even the finest speedware isn’t going to save you

Anyone attempting to dodge an attack made with this 
ammunition suffers a -2 penalty to their Skill Check.

explode.

Better Killing Through Chemistry
I was an architect of much suffering that your readership 
have endured, those being veterans impacted by the 
Second Central American War. I, Annabel Cruz, herein 
provide an account of my actions regarding my role in 
the horrific human chemical weapons testing undertaken 
as a joint effort by Biotechnica and the US military on 
unknowing American soldiers.
I do not seek forgiveness for what I have done. I do 
not deserve it. I fully believe the crimes I have committed 
damn my soul to burn in the fires of Hell. As I write this, 
I am sick with cancer, from which I will not recover. The 
truth of what happened, however, will not die with me.
In 2001, after graduating from Night City University 
with a degree in Pharmacology, I accepted a job offer 
to work for Biotechnica in a junior engineering role. It 
landed me in a botany laboratory, in which I sequenced 
plant genomes, looking for the next triticum vulgaris 
megasuavis. After two years of no results, I found myself 
forced to either accept a “promotion” and transfer to a 
newly formed confidential lab, or be fired. I regret saying 
yes, and I regret doing so enthusiastically. It was pathetic. 
At the time I told myself that the new role was a chance to 
save my career from failing before it ever had a chance 
to begin. In hindsight, this choice was my last chance to 
choose a path of righteousness. I chose poorly.
I was transferred to a black site in Colombia. It was an 
entirely Portuguese-speaking lab, staffed entirely by sci-
entists from the Rio de Janeiro Biotechnica offices. In the 
Night City office’s haste to get rid of me, they must have 
assumed I knew Portuguese, probably because those 
racist pricks never cared about me enough to ask. Or 
maybe they did, and thought it was funny. Being the only 
researcher that communicated using a translation chip, 
and an early 2000’s chip at that, I quickly earned the 
nickname “Robota” from my new colleagues. Inevitably, 
the close contact environment of the black site bonded us 
into a team. Perversely, while I committed the worst crimes 
of my life, I was happy. Happy to be a part of something 
that worked, to be a part of a winning team on the cutting 
edge of science.
The goal of our cell, Columbia-2, was to develop 
chemical weapons synthesized from local plants that 
could be used to silently attack an entrenched enemy 
force. The reasoning was that by working with locally 
available plants, the supply side of a potential conflict 
could be made more efficient. Most nerve gases have a 
notoriously short shelf life, and this was another problem 
local production could solve. We saw ourselves as scien-
tists for harm-reduction. We envisioned ourselves creating 
a kinder, gentler alternative to nerve and chlorine gas, 
something akin to a short-acting mustard gas that would 
have no lasting effects on the target. We were told that 
Columbia-1 was working on an airborne cleansing nano-
machine that would serve as the critical second half to our 
work, one that would reverse the effects of our weapons. 
Together, the final product would be a beautiful synthesis 
of chemistry and hy, a beautiful, ethical, modern weapon 
of war. I now know Columbia-1 never actually existed. 
They lied to ease our conscience.
As part of my research at Columbia-2, I sequenced the 
genome of various Columbian plants, in order to better 
understand how to mass-process chemical weapons 
from these locally abundant components, with the goal 
of eventual mass-synthesis of their unique tropane alka-
loids in a controlled environment. In this way, I became a 
mother to six terrible children by three separate fathers. 
Jimsonweed became the basis for Deliriant and Rime. 
Anti-Cerebral, Terrifier, and Mindfire were all gathered 
from Brugmansia Aurea. Finally, Mortalis, my greatest 
crime, was sourced from a plant that was hand-deliv-
ered to the site by “Columbia-1.” Our team was never 
able to conclusively identify the sample. I suspect it was 
thoroughly genetically modified, starting from somewhere 
in the nightshade family.
It was only later that I learned the truth. US soldiers 
would be exposed to aerosolized precursors to our 
weapons by their superiors as part of an agreement 
the military had with Biotechnica. These exposures were 
often framed as “reprisal attacks” by South American 
drug lords. Many died upon exposure, and many more

have had to live with terrible health outcomes, such as 
blindness, and paralysis, which the military was more 
than eager to “solve” with cybernetics as a part of their 
push toward a military focused on enhanced “cybersol-
diers.” When we received data back from these initial 
tests, we were told that Columbia-1 had performed the 
testing on invasive feral hogs. By the time we had used 
the data to produce weapons with a lower mortality 
rate, the damage had already been done. It continues 
to be done.
After the war, all six of my children quickly found their 
way into the secondary market. They have been present 
in some form in every armed conflict since, used on civil-
ians and combatants alike. They’ve been used to bomb 
hospitals, schools, churches, and gas densely packed 
slums. I used to keep track, so I could know the extent of 
my sins. I am no longer able to.
I’ll be dead about a week before they’re set to release. 
But you won’t. You are one of the only constituencies the 
powers that be even pay lip service to anymore. Only the 
veterans and soldiers of the future can stop this.
Combat Chemistry
When seconds matter, you need every advantage you 
can get to see victory. Turn to chemistry for a new edge.
 
X Multihypo
Cost: 500eb (Expensive)
Easy to use multibarreled drug distribution platform designed 
for military immunization work, which uses bursts of com-
pressed air to force multiple drugs at once through skin.
Allows the user to use an Action to administer a single dose of 
each drug loaded into the Multihypo to a target. Unlike a stan-
dard Airhypo, a Multihypo is too unwieldy to administer drugs 
to an unwilling conscious target. A Multihypo’s quad-reservoir 
can be loaded with four doses of four different medications 
(for a total of 16) as an Action. As a security feature, multiple 
doses of the same drug cannot be injected simultaneously 
should they be loaded into separate reservoirs.
 
X Arrowhypo Ammunition
Cost: 100eb (Premium) for 1
Ammo Types Available: Arrow

fully counterbalanced arrow, for use by specialist archers.

of a Street Drug. When using this ammunition, you deal no 
damage with your attack. Instead, anyone meat hit by your 
attack is injected with a dose of whatever street drug it was 
loaded with (if any). This ammunition may be recovered after 
firing just like a Basic Arrow.
 
X Airburst Ammunition
Cost: 125eb (Expensive) for 1
Ammo Types Available: Grenades, Rockets.
Sixteen coin-sized sensors in each unit of this gas-disper-
sal munition maximize the effective area of the payload 
to maximize tactical advantage.
In this way, I became  
a mother to six  
terrible children 
by three separate 
fathers. 
This letter is meant to raise an alarm. These chemicals 
are weapons of war, meant for the battlefield. They must 
not be allowed to be re-packaged, re-branded, and 
marketed for “security” use, as I understand Biotechnica 
is preparing to do. If it is true they are being “re-formu-
lated”, we may soon re-live the horrors of the “exposures” 
of 2003, but on the streets of your city, on invasive feral 
hogs, at a protest near you! 
There are no checks and balances in place to make 
sure that Biotechnica follows any kind of safety protocol 
or standard. And if Biotechnica’s “reformulation” amounts 
to nothing but a differently colored label with updated 
packaging, it puts military grade chemical weapons in the 
trustworthy hands of the police. Wouldn’t that be cost effec-
tive! Why spend millions watering down your product?

Cost per Dose: 50eb (Costly)
A modified form of the scopolamine contained within 
“Angel Trumpet” flowers, formulated to induce an extreme 
migraine in the subject. In many subjects, and particularly 
in those susceptible to migraines, Mindfire exposure does 
lasting damage to the hypothalamus. Useful for distract-
ing an enemy with pain.
Primary Effect
•	 Lasts 1 Minute (20 Rounds)
•	 User must succeed a DV15 Resist Torture/Drugs 
Check. A user without a Pain Editor that fails suffers 
1 damage directly to their HP at the end of each of 
their Turns, or one damage every 3 seconds if outside 
of combat. Damage from this effect cannot reduce 
the user below 1 HP.
Secondary Effect (DV15)
•	 Mindfire is not addictive.
•	 User without a Pain Editor who also failed to resist 
this drug’s Primary Effect suffers 1d6 Humanity Loss.
 
X Mortalis
Cost per Dose: 250eb (Expensive)
A sophisticated chemical weapon that simulates a 
cardiac arrest in the subject. To mimic stopping the 
heart, it triggers an extreme histamine response in 
the subject’s vertebral and carotid arteries, inflaming 
them to temporarily cease the flow of blood to the 
subject’s brain. Mortalis has been traditionally used in 
assassinations.

A chemical cocktail spiked with atropine and packaged 
in collagenolytic enzymes. By interacting with the sub-

Primary Effect
•	 Lasts 24 Hours

mitter-inhibitor enzyme works in symphony with a second 
enzyme that artificially re-wires brain pathways critical 
to threat memory, to weaponize the subject’s own fight-
or-flight response. Terrifier sees use today in the hands of 
military police, and soon, I fear, riot police everywhere.

attack using the Melee Weapon Skill, 5’s are treated 
as 6’s on your damage dice solely for the purpose of

Solo of Fortune 2045
Newbie Corner
If I had to start again, what would I do? Maybe you can 
take this as advice, but I wouldn’t recommend it — I’m 
a couple of years out of date. To start off, I’d strongly 
recommend you to settle down with a beautiful woman 
that loves you instead of doing this. If that’s not available 
to you, by all means, kill for a living.
I’m going to assume you’re starting from a foundation 
of real combat training and experience, but I’m a realist, 
so let’s assume it’s a single tour of military service or some 
police work. Maybe it’s a couple of years hitting for a 
gang. The streets are full of young killers these days, but 
that’s a dead end. Sooner rather than later, you’ve got to 
go pro if you want more out of life.
From street rat to street samurai, the only difference 
is standards. Professionals get paid. We don’t work for 
respect, territory, or revenge. We work for money. Money 
buys territory, helps you forget revenge. Work brings 
respect, in adoration and in fear. 
You’re going to need to invest money to get started, 
because before you can make the big bucks, you’ll need 
to survive your first year as a mercenary. That’s a couple 
of gunfights a month, for twelve months.
Think economically. You’re effectively a new business, 
so you need starting capital. Sell everything you own. 
Keep it in cash. If you can’t pull at least 10k together, you 
aren’t ready for the big time. Get rid of your SIN, your 
phone number. Move out of your place. Burn all docu-
mentation. Pay a Netrunner to burn your digital footprint. 
You can’t do this halfway. Attachments get you killed. Buy 
a backpack and stuff it full of cash. 
Here’s what I’d spend it on. Bodysculpting comes 
first. You need a new face. It will make everything else 
easier. Bribe the ripperdoc to wipe all the files. Get a new 
wardrobe. Your goal is nondescript. Avoid boots, vests, 
anything with camouflage. Nothing you’ve ever seen an 
off duty cop wear. Nothing flashy or identifiable. You 
want to look forgettable, like an accountant. Break up 
with your ripperdoc anyway. Get a new one you’ve never 
met before. You won’t believe how many people I know 
that got killed this way. It’s four, and one of those was 
kind of a double. Rippers love money, which makes them 
vulnerable to bribes. 
You need chrome, practical chrome. To operate full-
time as a Solo, you need enhanced antibodies, some 
form of subdermal plating, anti-dazzle, nasal filters, and 
if you’re smart, hardening for every cyberlimb you’ve 
got. Second priorities are muscle and bone lace, and 
reinforced joints for your limbs. One broken bone can 
turn a bad job into your last job. I got by with much less 
early on, sure, but the times have changed.
You need a new Fixer, too, because they’re even worse 
than ripperdocs at keeping secrets. Get this into your 
head: your Fixer isn’t your employer, and isn’t your friend, 
they’re a tool to get in contact with your next gig. I don’t 
care how many times they say they’re giving you a “friend 
price”. They all have a line, beyond which profit matters 
more than loyalty. That’s their real “friend price”. 
Secure a safe house in two different neighborhoods. 
Cargo containers with simple setups. Rent should run 
you about two grand a month for both. That’s the cost of 
doing business. You get bonus points if both safehouses 
One broken bone 
can turn a bad job 
into your last job. 
I got by with much 
less early on, sure, 
but the times have 
changed.

When things are going 
according to plan, a good 
rifle is your lifeblood, 
so treat yours like a 
princess.
are in rival gang territories. Your new neighbors won’t 
snitch on you to their own enemies, but just in case, never 
take the same route home twice.
Only take jobs that match your skill level. Every single 
“big job” you’re offered is a big trap. You aren’t special, 
or lucky. There’s a reason more established Solos passed 
up on that job. That job is either a good old fashioned 
bad idea, the trouble isn’t worth the fee, or word has 
gotten out that the employer wants someone they can kill 
off, because, no witnesses. Don’t work for corporations 
that have been backed into a corner, or even worse, are 
attacking one of their own. Palace intrigue is strict no-go. 
Those clients are guaranteed to turn on you after you’ve 
cleaned their house, nine times out of ten. In a way, it’s 
not like they’re even lying, because their nature is almost 
always laid bare in the job description. If they’re willing 
to betray their own people, they’re willing to betray you. 
When these clients are smart, they like to remain anon-
ymous. That’s why you should do your research before 
accepting any job. Don’t be a loose end.
When things are going according to plan, a good rifle 
is your lifeblood, so treat yours like a princess. You want 
something that isn’t unwieldy in a hallway but can handle 
distance. For those times when your princess leaves 
you high and dry, you need a backup. It should excel 
at solving problems, up close and personal and should 
always stay with you, even after a search. A shotgun in 
your cyberarm is a classic for a reason. You want armor 
penetration and high ammunition count, because some 
of these problems are Full Body Conversions. Invest in 
multiples, in case you get disarmed, and don’t get too 
attached. A gun can be like a fingerprint. Better to throw 
it in a smelter than be identified
I’ll leave you with one more piece of advice, something 
to think about once you’ve established your rep enough to 
turn down three or so “lifechanging opportunities” offered 
to you by your Fixer “buddy”. There’s one particular kind 
of job unique to our industry that’s guaranteed to shorten 
your career; never take a job to kill a fellow Solo. You 
should be ready, though. In the case you meet someone 
that has, remember that neither of you are special, or 
lucky. 
But maybe, if you’ve listened to this old man’s advice, 
it’ll be you writing an article like this in twenty years time, 
and not the choombatta hunting you. 
-M.B.
PS: R.A. don’t look for me. I won’t be there.
Solo Spotlight
These professional warriors have nominated by their 
comrades for a special shout out.
John Doe: A specialist in drone surveillance and 
covert ops, John Doe proves technology and the 
modern Solo can co-exist. Known for his cool temper 
and sharp sense of style, he is the perfect warrior 
to guide your crew through the toughest security 
challenges.
Mister Rieper: They say you could stare at Rieper 
for hours, yet forget what he looks like the second you 
turn your gaze away. He’s the sort of assassin other 
assassins tell ghost stories about. We hear he’s running 
a gang now, somewhere in Northern California.
Queen Morosha: In just a few short years, Queen 
Morosha has risen from the streets of Night City 
to leadership of her own company. The Queen is 
currently in Africa, under an exclusive employment 
contract of the Highrider Confederation.
Shaitan: Could the legendary FBC ninja still 
be alive? Word is he’s been spotted in Japan in 
connection with attacks on Arasaka assets. Send 
a message to Solo of Fortune > Shaitan Tracker if 
you spot him.
That’s it for this spotlight! If there’s a fellow Solo you 
want to shout out, submit your nomination to Solo of 
Fortune Solo Spotlight.

ACPAs
ACPAs
ACPAs in the Time of the Red
ACPA, Assisted Combat Power Armor, are combat envi-
ronment suits encased in Metalgear® plates built around a 
powered assist provided by a step-in Linear Frame, which 
enable their pilots to move them around despite their extreme 
weight. On the battlefield, they serve a narrow “siege” role. 
They are outclassed in speed and cost effectiveness per 
trooper by standard vehicles on open ground, but superior 
to them in narrow urban environments. 
The cost of ACPA suits, while lower now than during 
the 4th Corporate War, remains a large barrier of entry 
for most small militaries and mercenary companies. Add 
this to the fact that improvements in modern firepower, 
especially that of railguns, have lessened the once-invin-
cible protection provided by Metalgear® armor, ACPA 
find themselves at a precarious crossroads. 
In the 2040s, they are simultaneously too expensive for 
mass warfare, too niche for many operations, and reliant 
on a skilled pilot to bring out their full potential. ACPAs 
are better served as a force multiplier — boogeymen in 
theaters of urban and irregular war capable of maneu-
vering like a soldier but fighting like a vehicle.
Bootup Sequence
Getting into an unoccupied ACPA is an Action. As part 
of this Action, a user can connect themselves to the ACPA 
using two installations of Interface Plugs and become the 
ACPA’s Pilot. The vast majority of all ACPA are installed with 
Authorization Handshake Ports (see page 122) that will 
only accept “authorized users.” Getting out of an ACPA that 
isn’t destroyed is an Action. Forget passengers. An ACPA 
only has room inside for a single person.
Clamshell Protection
While inside an ACPA, the pilot’s body is encased tightly in 
a clamshell of armor, and is protected from outside attack. 
•	 It’s a tight fit and since the pilot guides the movement of the 
suit with their own limbs, any Armor Penalties from armor 
worn still applies and stacks with penalties imposed by 
the ACPA. Skip the flak vest and bring something lighter. 
•	 ACPA pilots are very much “locked in” their suits. 
They cannot make use of any cyberware installed 
in their body that needs to be popped up, plugged 
in, or otherwise physically engage with something 
outside of the suit to function.
•	 When piloting an ACPA, the pilot uses their own 
Skills just like they would outside of the suit. When 
a physical attack made from outside your suit would 
damage the pilot, their ACPA takes the damage 
instead, reducing its HP and SP instead of that of the 
person inside. The pilot’s  equipped armor does not 
lose SP when their ACPA’s SP is ablated.
•	 An ACPA cannot be grappled or thrown by anyone with 
a BODY 14 or lower and is immune to choke damage. 
The pilot cannot be grappled, thrown, or choked while 
inside a ACPA that isn’t destroyed.
•	 An ACPA’s head and limbs are not considered weak 
points, and ACPA are immune to Aimed Shots. 
Additionally, since ACPA are designed to reinforce a 
frail human body, Martial Arts Attacks and Special 
Moves don’t halve an ACPA’s SP. 
•	 ACPAs cannot suffer the effect of Critical Injuries. 
Instead, whenever a Critical Injury is scored on an 
ACPA the pilot suffers 5 damage directly to their HP 
due to neural feedback. This damage is reduced to 0 
for FBC pilots, due to their unique physiology.
•	 ACPA protect their pilots and their cyberware in a 
sealed and oxygenated environment, making them 
immune to EMP effects. All of an ACPA’s components, 
onboard weapons and cyberware are considered 
hardened (cannot be damaged or rendered inoperable 
by EMP effects like Microwaver pulses or Non-Black 
ICE Program effects) and cannot be set On Fire. 
•	 An ACPA’s sealed environment provides the pilot immu-
nity to electrocution, toxic gases, fumes, and all similar 
dangers that must contact skin, be inhaled, or injected to 
affect the target. Automatic light and noise compensation 
makes the pilot immune to blindness, deafness, or other 
effects caused by dangerous loud noises or flashes of 
light, like those of a Flashbang, Shrieker, or Growler.

ACPAs
ACPAs
Solo of Fortune 2045
•	 The sealed environment of the ACPA protects its pilot 
from radiation, extreme weather and environmental 
conditions, is watertight for underwater use, and is 
even suitable for use in space. An ACPA’s onboard 
oxygen tanks last up to 24 hours, and can be refilled 
in 1 hour via exposure to ambient air.
When an ACPA is Reduced to 0 HP
ACPAs are immune to Wound Threshold effects. An ACPA 
that is reduced to 0 HP is Destroyed and ceases to provide 
any protections to its pilot. A destroyed ACPA’s pilot is also 
unable to use any of its Onboard Weapons/Cyberware, 
or use its Linear Frame. The one upside to your ACPA being 
Destroyed is that you don’t need to use an Action to exit a 
destroyed ACPA … you simply walk right through the new 
big hole your enemy made in the side of it.
Linear Frame Core
All ACPA are built around a Linear Frame that the user 
can take advantage of while they pilot the ACPA. This 
increase in BODY does not affect the user’s HP or change 
their Death Save. If a user has a more powerful linear 
frame than the ACPA they are piloting, their BODY 
remains at the higher value. 
The arms of an ACPA are treated as cyberarms for the 
purposes of damaging cover, and the legs of an ACPA 
are treated as cyberlegs for the purposes of falling.
Wielding Weapons
An ACPA can wield weapons as if it were a human. All 
normal rules apply, except an ACPA can wield any two-
handed weapon with one hand except for bows.
Onboard Weapons & Cyberware
In addition to wielding any weapons held in their ACPA’s 
hands, the pilot of an ACPA may also make use of their 
ACPA’s Onboard Weapons and Cyberware. Wielding 
onboard weapons doesn’t take up the use of the ACPA’s 
hands. Any weapon installed as an Onboard Weapon 
or wielded in an ACPA’s hands is considered to be con-
nected to the pilot via an appropriate number of Interface 
Plugs for it to fully function, and if it’s an onboard weapon, 
it cannot be disarmed. In addition to any cyberware 
installed as Onboard Cyberware on an ACPA, all ACPA 
contain a sensor suite that operates like a Radar/Sonar 
Implant (see CP:R page 363) that has been modified 
to scan out to 100 m/yds.
Tuned to Perfection
ACPAs are designed with tradeoffs in mind for the battlefield 
roles that they seek to fill. ACPAs, by themselves, do not have 
a REF, DEX, or MOVE STAT. The pilot’s are used instead. 
While piloting an ACPA, a user’s REF, DEX, and MOVE may 
be positively or negatively affected by the suit.
Maximum Operation Time
ACPA control feeds are difficult on the pilot’s senses, not 
to mention their brain. A pilot who spends more than 
4 hours piloting an ACPA on a given day suffers a -1 
penalty to all Checks until they complete a day of rest. 
FBC pilots and non-FBC pilots with Rank 4 or higher in 
Moto or Interface are better attuned to the stress and do 
not suffer this penalty.
All pilots, no matter their Role Ability or FBC status, who 
spend more than 8 hours piloting an ACPA on a given 
day suffer a -2 penalty to all Checks until they complete 
a day of rest.
No Bullet Dodging in an ACPA … Unless
A pilot can’t dodge bullets in an ACPA unless they meet one 
of the following requirements. If they do, they can dodge 
bullets, even if they couldn’t outside of an ACPA:
•	 The ACPA has a MOVE bonus of +3 or higher.
•	 The pilot has a REF 8 or higher after bonuses/penal-
ties, a Reflex Co-Processor, and the ACPA has no REF 
Penalty.
•	 The pilot has a DEX 8 or higher after bonuses/
penalties, and the ACPA has no REF, DEX, or MOVE 
Penalty.
•	 The pilot has a Combat Awareness Rank of 6 or 
higher.

ACPAs
ACPAs
Modular Mecha Repairs
An ACPA is too complicated to be Jury Rigged by a Tech. 
However, ACPAs are designed for heavy use and relative 
ease of repair. The DV and amount of time it takes to repair 
a damaged/destroyed ACPA is based on the amount of 
damage it has sustained and the Check is made using the 
Weaponstech Skill.  On a failed roll, halfway through the 
repair, the mechanic realizes that they’ll have to start the repair 
again from scratch. Whenever an ACPA is repaired, all of its 
Onboard Weapons/Cyberware are repaired, and the SP of 
its Metalgear® clamshell and its HP are restored to maximum.
 
X ACPA Repair Time
Damage	
DV	
Time
Minor Damage	
  9	
4 Hours 
Less than Full HP
Major Damage	
13	
1 Day 
Less than 1/2 HP
Destroyed	
  17	
1 Week 
0 HP
Building a ACPA
To build an ACPA, first select its ACPA Linear Frame, then 
its ACPA Shell, and then decide how the ACPA is tuned in 
Maneuverability, Hydraulics, Acceleration, and Weight. 
You are required to make a selection, A or B, for each of 
these four tunings. Every decision made when building an 
ACPA impacts its HP and its REF, DEX, MOVE Bonuses/
Penalties, so keep track of each as you go. 
Add up to 6 Onboard Weapons to the ACPA. You 
may install up to 6 pieces of cyberware in an ACPA. 
Additional Linear Frames and cyberware that grants/
restores SP cannot be installed. You cannot install cyber-
ware only available to FBCs in an ACPA. When installed 
in an ACPA, all cyberware installation prerequisites 
are ignored, and all cyberware installed is treated as 
paired. Adding cyberware with open slots does not 
increase the total number of pieces that can installed.
To find the market Price and Availability of your ACPA, 
begin with a base of 4,000eb. Add the cost of the 
ACPA Linear Frame, the cost of the ACPA Shell, the cost 
of each Onboard Weapon and Cyberware, and then 
add an additional 500eb per Onboard Weapon and 
Cyberware. Your ACPA’s final Availability Rating is equal 
to that of its most difficult to acquire component.
A Tech cannot upgrade, fabricate, or invent an entire 
ACPA. Instead, they must use their Role Ability to upgrade, 
fabricate, or invent each of an ACPA’s components sepa-
rately. The components should then be assembled using 
the following rules.
When fabricating an ACPA Linear Frame or ACPA Shell 
component, the cost is half the cost of the Frame or half the 
cost of the Shell, and is done with the Weaponstech Skill. 
Final assembly of an ACPA from loose components/dis-
assembly of an ACPA into its loose components is a DV24 
Weaponstech Check, and takes 24 hours. When assem-
bling an ACPA, a Tech must spend a base fee of 2,000eb 
but does not have to spend 500eb per Onboard Weapon 
and/or Cyberware piece. Adding, removing, or swapping 
out any number of Onboard Weapons/Cyberware is a 
DV21 Weaponstech Check, and takes 24 hours.
On a failure, halfway through the process, the mechanic 
realizes they need to start again from scratch, but the com-
ponents are not lost.

ACPAs
 
X Example: Creating an ACPA
Jeremy decides to order a new ACPA from a manufacturer. 
First, he chooses an ACPA Linear Frame and a ACPA 
Shell. He selects a Sigma Frame that comes with 22 
HP, BODY 12, and +1 MOVE, and a Salvaged Shell 
that comes with 14 SP, + 2 HP, and -1 MOVE. 
Together, before tuning, his ACPA has 24 HP and 12 
BODY. It’s +1 and -1 MOVE cancel each other out. 
Jeremy isn’t done, however. He must tune the ACPA, 
selecting either Option A or Option B for each of the four 
tunings: Acceleration, Hydraulics, Maneuverability, and 
Weight. 
For Acceleration, he selects Option B, which comes 
with -1 DEX. 
For Hydraulics, he selects Option A, which comes 
with -1 SP. 
For Maneuverability, he selects Option A, which 
comes with -7 HP. 
For Weight, he selects Option A, which comes with 
+7 HP. 
After Tuning, his ACPA has 13 SP, 24 HP, and -1 DEX. 
Now comes Onboard Weapons and Cyberware. 
Jeremy decides on a Highland Defense Stickybomb 
Launcher valued at 1,300eb and a Skate Foot valued 
at 500eb. Since all onboard cyberware is automatically 
considered paired, Jeremy doesn’t need two Skate Feet. 
Jeremy now needs to calculate the price of his ACPA
•	 Base Cost: 4,000eb
•	 ACPA Linear Frame: 2,000eb
•	 ACPA Shell: 1,000eb
•	 Highland Defense Stickybomb Launcher: 1,300eb
•	 Skate Foot: 500eb
•	 Additional Cost per Onboard Weapon (x1): 500eb 
•	 Additional Cost per Cyberware (x1): 500eb
The final cost for Jeremy to buy this ACPA is 9,800eb, 
and it is Luxury availability, as its hardest to acquire part 
(Sigma ACPA Linear Frame) is Luxury. 
If Jeremy was a Tech and he wanted to build this ACPA 
himself, he first would have to acquire or fabricate all 
of its parts separately (fabricating the Frame and Shell 
would be half the listed cost), spend 2,000eb, and then 
succeed at a DV24 Weaponstech Skill Check that takes 
24 hours. He doesn’t have to spend 500eb per Onboard 
Weapon and Cyberware, because he’s doing the work 
himself. On a failure, halfway through the process, he 
would realize he needed to start again from scratch, but 
the components would not be lost.
Jeremy’s Custom ACPA 
Cost: 9,800eb (Luxury)
An ACPA, custom-built by Jeremy.
24 HP • 13 SP • BODY 12 • -1 DEX
Linear Frame: Sigma
Shell: Salvaged Shell
Onboard Weapons: Highland Defense Stickybomb 
Launcher
Onboard Cyberware: Skate Foot
ACPA LinGo
•	 Aperture-Based (AB): A low-quality ACPA, 
often lacking onboard sensors.
•	 Critical: Very bad. “I’ve got critical news.”
•	 Kipple: Battlefield rubble.
•	 Lacehead: A pilot on combat drugs, especially 
an addict.
•	 Monkey: An newbie ACPA pilot.
•	 Nudie: A personnel target without an ACPA.
•	 Pearl: A personnel target in an ACPA.
•	 Scrapheap: A heavily damaged or destroyed ACPA.
•	 SSR: Suit Systems Read. The data from an ACPA’s 
internal monitors.

capable of firing its own unique Armor-Piercing Ammunition

GEORGIA ON MY MIND
A Mercenary Level Screamsheet Mission
P
layer Information
p
l a
y
e
r
i
n
f

r
m
a
t
i o n
SovOil is looking to hire a team of mercenaries to do 
some dirty work. They want to kidnap the daughter 
of a leader of Petrochem-backed Georgian rebels 
to encourage them to cease their attacks on SovOil 
holdings in the oil-rich American South. The megacorp 
will hire anyone to do the delicate job, no matter how 
ill suited they may be. It does not care for a millisecond 
about the safety of the woman they plan on capturing, 
and this disregard is evident. SovOil plans on hiring 
separate teams to capture other children and loved 
ones of the remaining rebel leaders simultaneously. 
The megacorp’s money, however, is good, and the 
job, however immoral, seems simple enough on face 
value. The job pays 5,000eb per person, and comes 
with an operational budget of 5,000eb per person. All 
just to kidnap one little girl, home alone.
Any team that accepts the job will receive this briefing 
from a ski-masked SovOil Deniable Operations Chief 
that introduces himself as “Revmir Babich”, obviously 
not his real name.
“Your job is to extract one “Kelly McAlister” from her 
family’s fortified compound in the Panola Mountain wil-
derness. This job is part of a series of simultaneous oper-
ations in the area. It is possible that she will know you are 
coming, but we have it on good authority that she’ll be 
completely alone on the property. We’ll transport you 
and your vehicles to Atlanta by ocean, where we’ve set 
up an operations base on the fourth and fifth floor of the 
Wright Hotel. You’ll have 48 hours to acclimatize before 
the operation begins. If you encounter local resistance 
or law enforcement, you are on your own. You will not 
receive backup and SovOil will not claim you. Your 
team will be paid upon the delivery of Kelly, alive, to 
the Wright Hotel. If she dies, you’ll receive no payment. 
Either way, we’ll transport you back. But let’s not kill the 
kid, yeah? That’s not what anyone wants here.”
G
amemaster Notes
g
a
m
e
m
a
s
t
e
r
n
o
t
e
s
The Wright Hotel is a Headquarters with 16 beds, 
Morale Boost 4, Upgraded Lockup (6 cells), Upgraded 
Medbay & a Workstation.
Kelly is indeed home alone, but is by no means 
undefended. SovOil’s intelligence on this operation is 
surface level and missing the mark on several important 
ideas. Any investigation-minded Edgerunner can find 
this information with relative ease (DV15). 
For one, Kelly is 32, not exactly a little girl, and has 
two masters degrees: one in robotics and the other in 
rare earth mining engineering. SovOil has done only 
a cursory search, returning the only images that can 
be found of her on the Data Pool — the ones from the 
various robotics fairs she won in high school. Her name 
appears on two patents for mining robots:
1. The Rock Razer: An Efreet-piloted Ground 
Drone w/ EQ Very Heavy Melee Weapon, Popup 
Shield, Observation Camera, and 11 SP. 
2. Mineral Softener: An Efreet-piloted Large Air 
Drone w/ EQ Flamethrower, Grenade Launcher w/ 
Incendiary Grenades, and 11 SP. 
Kelly is a private person, and not much else can be 
found on her, unless some digs deep (DV24), in which 
case they discover that after a recent series of exotic 
surgeries she appears crocodilian. This is the reason 
Kelly is currently on her own — most of her friends and 
family are wary of the bodysculpt and do not want to 
be near her right now.
Kelly’s father, Irvine McAlister, is the financial brain 
that stretches every dollar his Georgian Rebel group 
receives from Petrochem. While he doesn’t understand 
his daughter’s “attitude issues,” he is proud of her scien-
tific accomplishments, loves her, and stands up for her 
when she’s called “cold blooded,” at family gatherings. 
He also loves rolling up his sleeves and doing renova-
tion projects with her around the house.
The McAlister Ranch is located on 10 acres in the 
Panola Mountain wilderness. It consists of a main 
house and a guest house, both of which border a 
small man-made fishing lake. 
Main House
The main house has two floors and a basement. 
1st Floor Rooms (defenses in parenthesis)
A beautiful wraparound Porch (Laser Grid), Bathroom 
(Stun Panels), Den (Stun Panels), Dining Room (Tip 
Floor), Kitchen (Wall Punchers), Laundry Room.

145
GEORGIA ON MY MIND
2nd Floor Rooms (defenses in parenthesis)
A stately Balcony overlooking the entrance, Main 
Bathroom (Goop), Two Bedrooms, Main 
Bedroom, Walk-in Closet (Ceiling Punchers) with 
a chute to the basement. 
Basement
The basement is a robot workshop and armory, con-
tains the ranch’s NET Architecture. It connects to the 
bomb shelter via a secret passage (DV15 to locate).
Guest House
The guest house has a single floor and a basement.
1st Floor Rooms (defenses in parenthesis)
Bathroom, Bedroom (Stun Panels), Billiards 
Room (Electrical Flooring), Small Kitchen (Goop).
Basement
The basement is empty and connects to the bomb shelter 
via a secret passage (DV15 to locate).
Bomb Shelter
The bomb shelter underground has been converted into a 
two lane bowling alley as part of Irvine’s hobby project. It 
is guarded by 2 Automated Turrets that fire bowling balls 
(5d6 damage). It also has a secret door (DV17 to locate) 
leading to a flooded tunnel that opens out in the lake.
Begin Operation
When the operation begins at sundown on the third day, 
Kelly is aware the Crew is coming, and has her defenses 
set up. She knows that if she can survive until morning she 
will be saved by Petrochem-backed mercenaries (a Rival 
Crew). She also has the following at her disposal:
A number of Rock Razer drones equal to the number of 
Edgerunners. A number of Mineral Softener drones equal 
to the number of Edgerunners. Each drone carries its own 
NET Architecture. Four Thermal Decoys in the shape of 
Rock Razer drones set up at various points on the property.
Should the Crew betray their employer at any point, 
SovOil will send two waves of Rival Crews to dis-
patch both them and Kelly, and Petrochem’s incoming 
crew (sent to rescue Kelly) will be dispatched off-cam-
era. In this case, Petrochem will end up paying out the 
Crew  what SovOil would have if Kelly survives.

TROUBLE IN TANZANIA
A Mercenary Level Screamsheet Mission

The Crew responds to the following Hiring Hall ad in 
Solo of Fortune magazine:
Tanzania needs troops! Unprecedented recent aggression 
by the European Space Council at the Kilimanjaro mass driver 
must be met with strong resistance. Any soldier that fights for 
us, showing solidarity with our people who have poured their 
hopes, dreams, and labor into our mass driver, will be richly 
appreciated. Join us in defending the future of the stars!
Any Crew that accepts the job will be invited to an 
open recruitment meeting at the future Night City 
Spaceport. Although the meeting takes place in a large 
meeting room, the crew are the only people to arrive, 
which slightly unsettles the Tanzanian recruiter. They 
pass out Non-Disclosure Agreements to the Crew. If the 
Edgerunners sign, they’ll be given the following briefing.
“Welcome, brave soldiers. I am an Ambassador to 
the ArushaMoshi Metroplex, Joshua Mwambe. I thank 
you for coming to this meeting. I am not a political 
animal, nor a military general. I am only a patriot for a 
free and independent Tanzania. I will, in the interest of 
expediency, cut to the heart of the matter. Tanzania is 
not at war, but our war-readiness is being tested by the 
European Space Council. They have in recent weeks 
sent several proxy-attacks to probe our defenses, all 
using external contractors, all which we have repelled. 
For these they have denied involvement, but we are not 
so stupid and must respond. 
“Your mission will be to defend the Kilimanjaro 
Mass Driver’s anti-air battery on the western face of 
Mawenzi Peak from what we expect to be an esca-
lation of these hostilities as a form of pressure during 
upcoming negotiations with the European Space 
Council. Due to the extreme mountain terrain, we 
expected to enemy to send ACPA units.
“Please send exact specifications for what your team 
requires, and we will have it waiting for you. For each 
of you, we have an Operational Budget of 25,000eb, 
and Fixers and Techs to source or craft whatever you 
need, including customized ACPAs. Your pay for this 
special defensive operation shall be 9,000eb per 
person, and a free round trip to Africa. The people of 
Tanzania are generous to our heroes!”

The situation in the ArushiMoshi Metroplex is complex. 
The European Space Council (ESC) has turned the 
region into a secret battlefield for months now to 
make way for a planned military invasion that would 
ultimately grant them full control of the Kilimanjaro 
Mass Driver. The ESC has pulled out every trick in 
the book to soften up Tanzania, including sabotaging 
communications, paying and pressuring journalists into 
publishing hit pieces, setting off large scale EMPs, and 
even resorting to terrorist attacks in popular shopping 
neighborhoods in the Metroplex. 
The people of Tanzania have not been fooled by 
the European Space Council. Anti-ESC protests are a 
daily occurrence, and many who are in the streets are 
pushing the government to full deny European countries 
access to the mass driver in response to their crimes. 
Existing hate groups are taking advantage of the 
protests to attack European immigrant communities in 
the Metroplex, which has resulted in confusing clashes 
between the Tanzanian military and “protesters.”
The ArushiMoshi Metroplex
The Crew arrives at the Kilimanjaro International 
Airport in a military plane one month before the 
negotiations begin, and are asked to change into 
Tanzanian military fatigues. While wearing these 
fatigues, Edgerunners will be treated as extensions of 
the state — one not everyone spilling out into the streets 
of Tanzania is happy with. 
In addition to the fatigues, each Edgerunner receives 
an official-looking signed letter of support from 
Ambassador Mwambe (a powerful social tool) and 
a Swahili language Skill Chip. The Crew is 
also given use of a Groundcar w/ Heavy Chassis 
& Seating Upgrade and access to paid-for hotel 
accommodations in the Moshi Financial District.
The Crew has two weeks to enjoy visiting Tanzania on 
the government’s dime before being transported to the 
Marangu Military Base. There, they will be given access to 
their ACPAs and gear requisitioned via Operational Budget.
The Marangu Military Base is a Headquarters 
with 12 beds, Lockup (1 cell), Medbay, Upgraded 
Training Area Workshop, and Workstation.


---

<!-- page 148 -->

147
TROUBLE IN TANZANIA
This month (two weeks in the city and two weeks at the 
base) is your opportunity as a GM to flex your improvisa-
tional skills and play in a setting that isn’t Night City. Key 
to achieving the feel of being in a different country will be 
roleplaying a different culture, one less cynical than Night 
City, but still more cynical than our present day world, 
exploring the political unrest in the streets, and the different 
ways the Edgerunners are treated in and out of uniform, 
especially if they look or act “foreign.”
Pull on all the Lifepath data that can stretch interna-
tionally, like Exec companies, Fixer and Media contacts, 
Nomad packs, and Rockerboy fanbases. If the Crew 
gets into combat, make sure it either ties back into the 
unrest or brings them in conflict with ESC-hired saboteurs. 
One Week Until the Negotiations
The week before the negotiations, a Rival Crew, hired by 
the European Space Council, will attack the Edgerunners 
while they are training in an area just outside the base. 
This Rival Crew isn’t interested in fighting to the death, only 
injuring them and escaping. If the Edgerunners manage to 
capture any of the assailants, they have a Resist Torture/
Drugs Base of 16, and can reveal the exact nature of the 
upcoming attack to the players. The Tanzanians will want 
these prisoners alive. Should the Crew kill a captured 
enemy, their pay will be docked 1,000eb each.
During the Negotiations
During the week of the negotiations, there are three 
major attacks on the anti-air battery.
Anti-Air Battery
Remote-operated w/Combat Number 16•15 SP•100 
HP•EQ Rocket Launcher w/ Long Tube & 50 capacity. 
The emplacement can be retracted underground with 
3 Actions. While retracted, it cannot be attack or be 
damaged. Cannot be Jury Rigged during combat.
The first attack is a long distance assault on the facil-
ity, which seeks to damage the crew’s ACPA suits from 
range. It also makes use of two attack helicopters and 
a Magus ACPA, hoping to bait the anti-air battery into 
use so that it can be targeted by the ACPA’s railgun.
The second attack, which occurs 10 hours later 
on the following day, is a full frontal assault using the 
Militech Commando ACPAs and a portion of the 
Hardened Security Operatives in a Groundcar, 
decided upon by the GM. Before this attack, the Crew’s 
position is reinforced by two military special operatives 
(Hardened Security Officers with Shotguns & Explosive 
Slugs). This is also when any outside backup, perhaps 
engaged during the period before the attacks, arrives 
to help.
The third attack, which occurs 3 days after the 
last, is a desperate operation that sends all remain-
ing units, including the Bombardier ACPA, at 
the anti-air battery. The enemy begins to flee after all 
their ACPAs have retreated or been eliminated. If no 
enemy escaped the second battle alive/uncaptured, 
the enemy’s morale was broken, and this third attack 
never comes.
If the Crew is victorious, they are offered Tanzanian 
citizenship, and however things go down, is paid and is 
returned to whatever non-European airport they wish.
ESC-Hired Mercenary Forces 
See Hardened DLCs for Stat Blocks
These forces are intelligent and will retreat if it makes tac-
tical sense. Any forces reduced to under 1/2 HP during 
the first and second attack won’t participate in the third.
•	 A number of Hardened Security Operatives equal 
to twice the number of Edgerunners + 3. Add Battle Barrel 
to their Assault Rifles. Add Armor Piercing Ammunition.
•	 Cloudburster: Hardened Lieutenant Reclaimer 
Chief Thunder. Add Combat Awareness Rank 6,

•	 Sniper Eagle: Hardened Lieutenant Reclaimer Chief

•	 Iron Tiger: Hardened Lieutenant Security Officer 
Siege. Add Combat Awareness Rank 6, Sigma 
External Linear Frame, Skill Base 16 in Martial Arts 
(Judo), Reflex Co-Processor.
•	 Helicopter (x2) w/Bulletproof Glass & Rocket Pod.
•	 Groundcar (x2) w/ Armored Chassis, Bulletproof

Mount with Techtronika Russia BMG-500 & High

SovOil Bombardier (x1), Tsunami Arms Magus (x1)

Night City’s voracious, choomba! It needs more places, more people, 
more things. More! More! More! It’s never satisfied and we know you ar-
en’t either. Your campaigns can always use a little something extra to 
push it right over the Edge. That’s what Interface RED is all about. In this 
fifth volume you’ll find a collection of short articles custom-crafted to im-
prove your Cyberpunk RED experience. We’ve featured many of these 
articles on our website as free DLC for you to download to your data 
storage device of choice but this is the first time we’ve collected them into a 
single book! Plus, we’ve thrown in a new, exclusive article, adding mercenary 
level play, military grade gear, and power armor to your campaign!
Volume 5 of Interface RED adds the following new content to your 
Cyberpunk RED game:
Breaking Your Stuff: A robust optional system that expands item 
damage with durability, maintenance, and malfunctions.
Chasing the Rabbit: Roller derby in the Time of the Red! Explore the 
history of the sport and bring it into your game with full rules.
All About Agents: A complete history of the communicator in every-
one’s pocket, how to hack them, and the hottest Apps to install.
Toggle’s Temple: A collection of seventy-two ranged weapons 
covering, providing the lore and mechanics you need for a Friday 
Night Firefight.
Did Someone Say Murder?: An optional investigation system that 
enables your campaign to better focus on mysteries and looking for clues.
Your New Best Friend: Cyberpets! Make ample space in your 
cargo container for a Forever Turtle™, Data Rabbit™, or something 
more exotic.
Screamsheet Generator: Create your own plot points and news 
article. A lifepath for current events, and a savior for GMs everywhere.
12 Days of REDmas: Lore and stats for twelve items that have shown 
up in previous Cyberpunk RED art. Add them to your game!
Solo of Fortune 2045: A massive mini-supplement stuffed full 
of weapons, attachments, ammunition, explosives, machine guns, 
ACPA (Assisted Combat Power Armor), and plenty of Solo-focused 
lore to celebrate the introduction of Mercenary Level Campaigns to 
Cyberpunk RED.
